# Lapidary: Implementation Plan

This file is the design log, written for the coding agents that build the app. Users read `README.md`; developers read `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

Written 2026-09-08. This plan is meant to be executed by a Claude Code session phase by phase. Read `CLAUDE.md` first for repo rules.

## 0. What we are building

A Windows desktop app (Electron) for reviewing Claude-suggested copyedits to novel chapters written as Markdown in an Obsidian vault. The author sees their original text double-spaced, with each suggested change floating above the words it would replace. Each change can be accepted or dismissed at any time, in either direction. Dismissed changes stay visible but faded. Accepted changes swap places with the original, which becomes the faded ghost. Paragraph-level comments (continuity, plot, flow) appear in a side panel. Claude is called from inside the app using the author's own Claude subscription; since Phase 5c OpenAI can answer instead, on an API key the author pastes into Settings (section 5.0). The author can also write their own edits, which become suggestions like any other. Nothing is ever written into an existing manuscript file except through an explicit Save action; the only other file operation is creating a new chapter, which cannot touch a file that already exists.

## 1. Locked decisions

| Decision              | Choice                                                                                                                                                                      | Why                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| --------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Shell                 | Electron 44 + electron-vite 5 + Vite 7 + React 19 + TypeScript (see Appendix C.1)                                                                                                              | Node main process hosts the Agent SDK, file I/O, and watcher with no friction.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Claude access         | `@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk` (0.3.x), single-shot `query()` with all tools removed, custom system prompt, structured JSON output                                        | It is the surface Anthropic bills against a Claude subscription. As of June 15 2026 Anthropic paused the planned separate credit pool, so Agent SDK and third-party app usage draws from the subscription's normal usage limits. An `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in the environment works as a drop-in alternative because the SDK reads it first. |
| Auth handling         | The app never reads, stores, or proxies credentials. The user's own Claude Code reads the login it wrote itself (C.42; the app ships no Claude Code since `cli.1`). | Required by Anthropic's policy; sign-in must complete through Anthropic's own flow.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Primary Claude output | A change list: `{changes: [{paragraph, before, after, category, reason}], comments: [...]}`                                                                                 | The author's edits are a few words at a time. Short exact quotes anchor reliably, output is tiny, and every change carries a reason and category. Larger issues become comments by design.                                                                                                                                                |
| Secondary input       | Full-text diff (a corrected copy of the open chapter, pasted or loaded from a file)                                                                                          | Needed for "Submit manual review" (7.5) and for imports. Same hunk model, same UI.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Folders               | User picks a manuscript folder and a reviews folder, anywhere on disk                                                                                                       | Inside or outside the vault does not matter. Reviews are `.json`, which Obsidian does not index.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Sync                  | File watcher + content hashes + text-anchored hunks that re-anchor on external change                                                                                       | Obsidian and this app can both touch the file; neither may clobber the other silently.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Model                 | Setting, default `default`: no `--model` flag, so Claude Code uses the account's own default (`modelArgs`, `setup.1`). A settings file that names a model keeps it, and a new user on a Pro plan gets `sonnet` preselected once (Appendix D 12). List populated from Claude Code's `initialize` reply, each entry labelled with its version and grouped by family (`shared/ai/claudeModels.ts`, round 5). Effort setting, default `high`. | What Default means depends on the account: Opus on Max, Sonnet on Pro. On a Pro plan the review dialog notes that Opus models, Default included, use the limits fastest. |
| OpenAI access (5c)    | Official `openai` package (7.15.x), Responses API, strict `json_schema` text format, streamed. API key stored encrypted with `safeStorage`, `OPENAI_API_KEY` as fallback.   | OpenAI's documented route for a third-party app is an API key billed per token (Appendix A). The key is the one credential the app stores, so it is encrypted, main-process only, and never shown again (CLAUDE.md rule 2).                                                                                                               |

## 2. Repository layout

```
ai-grammar-checker/
  package.json
  electron.vite.config.ts
  tsconfig*.json
  src/
    main/                 Electron main process (Node)
      index.ts            window creation, app lifecycle
      ipc.ts              IPC handlers, typed against src/shared/ipc.ts
      files.ts            list .md and .txt, read (EOL/BOM detection), atomic write, exclusive create, sha256
      watcher.ts          chokidar on the manuscript folder, own-write suppression
      reviews.ts          review store: create, load latest, save decisions
      providers/          the AI provider layer (5.0)
        types.ts          ReviewProvider interface, hooks, ProviderResult
        registry.ts       provider lookup by id, no Electron
        index.ts          the real registry, in-flight requests for Cancel
        claude.ts         Agent SDK call, progress, error mapping, account info
        openai.ts         Responses API call, streaming, error mapping, model list
      secrets.ts          the OpenAI key: safeStorage-encrypted secrets.json
      settings.ts         settings JSON in app.getPath('userData')
    preload/
      index.ts            contextBridge exposing a typed `api` object only
    renderer/             React UI, no Node access
      index.html, main.tsx, App.tsx
      components/         FileList, ReviewPane, HunkSpan, HunkTooltip, MarginNotes, SidePanel, Toolbar,
                          EditPopover, ReviewDialog, NewChapterDialog, ManualReviewDialog,
                          SettingsDialog, SetupScreen, StatusBar
      state/              store (zustand), selectors, keyboard bindings
      styles/
    shared/               pure TypeScript, no Node or DOM imports, fully unit-tested
      types.ts            Hunk, ReviewComment, Review, Settings, enums
      branding.ts         APP_NAME and APP_DATA_DIR, the only place the name is written
      edit.ts             page layout of a span, paragraph-edit planning, supersede, manual hunks
      ipc.ts              channel names and payload types
      text/
        paragraphs.ts     split into paragraphs with offsets, frontmatter detection
        eol.ts            detect and restore CRLF/LF, BOM, trailing newline
      diff/
        wordDiff.ts       full-text -> hunks (paragraph align, then word diff)
        anchor.ts         change list -> hunks (exact, neighbor, fuzzy, stale)
        compose.ts        base text + hunk decisions -> output text
        rebase.ts         re-anchor an existing review onto changed text
      prompt/
        system.ts         static instructions + guidelines assembly
        schema.ts         zod schema for Claude's output and JSON Schema export (draft-07)
        chapter.ts        render numbered paragraphs for the user message
      ai/
        openai.ts         model filter and default, effort map, key wording (no zod: the renderer imports it)
        openaiReply.ts    strict-schema transform and reply parsing (carries the zod schema)
        pricing.ts        OpenAI price table for the cost estimate
  resources/
    GUIDELINES.default.md copied into the manuscript folder on first use if absent
  test/                   vitest, mirrors src/shared
  PLAN.md  CLAUDE.md
```

Dependencies as installed on 2026-09-08: electron 44.3.0, electron-vite 5.0.0, vite 7.3.6, @vitejs/plugin-react 5.2.0, react 19.2.8, zod 4.5.4, diff 9.0.0 (jsdiff), diff-match-patch 1.0.5, chokidar 5.0.0, @anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk 0.3.266 (bundles a 219 MB native win32-x64 binary as an optional dependency; upgraded to 0.3.283 on 2026-09-27, whose bundled CLI knows Opus 5.5). Add zustand, electron-builder, vitest, eslint, prettier, fast-check. See Appendix C.1 for why Vite is 7 rather than 8.

## 3. Data model (`src/shared/types.ts`)

```ts
export type ChangeCategory =
  | 'spelling'
  | 'typo'
  | 'homophone'
  | 'grammar'
  | 'agreement'
  | 'tense'
  | 'punctuation'
  | 'word-choice'
  | 'redundancy'
  | 'awkward'
  | 'consistency'
  | 'formatting'
  | 'other';

export type CommentCategory =
  | 'consistency'
  | 'plot'
  | 'flow'
  | 'pacing'
  | 'clarity'
  | 'repetition'
  | 'pov'
  | 'style'
  | 'suggestion'
  | 'other';

export type HunkStatus = 'pending' | 'accepted' | 'dismissed';
export type AnchorQuality = 'exact' | 'fuzzy' | 'ambiguous' | 'stale';

export interface Hunk {
  id: string;
  start: number; // char offset in review.base.text (LF-normalized)
  end: number; // exclusive
  baseSide: 'original' | 'replacement'; // which text currently occupies [start,end) in base
  original: string; // may be '' for a pure insertion
  replacement: string; // may be '' for a pure deletion
  paragraph: number; // paragraph index at anchor time, for display and navigation
  category?: ChangeCategory;
  reason?: string;
  source: 'claude' | 'diff' | 'manual'; // 'manual' is an edit the author typed (7.4)
  status: HunkStatus;
  anchor: AnchorQuality; // 'stale' hunks are listed in the side panel, not inline
  edited?: boolean; // a model suggestion whose wording the author rewrote
  supersededBy?: string; // id of the manual hunk whose span swallowed this one
}

export interface ReviewComment {
  id: string;
  paragraph: number | null; // null when Claude gave no usable anchor
  quote?: string; // short excerpt Claude referenced, if any
  category: CommentCategory;
  text: string; // may include a suggested rewrite
  status: 'open' | 'resolved';
}

export interface Review {
  version: 1;
  id: string; // <chapter-slug>--<ISO timestamp>
  sourcePath: string; // absolute path of the chapter; the review belongs to exactly this file (files.1, C.45)
  createdAt: string;
  updatedAt: string;
  decidedAt?: string; // files.1: last decision (suggestion, comment, save); picks the current review
  base: { text: string; hash: string; eol: 'lf' | 'crlf'; bom: boolean; trailingNewline: boolean };
  generator: {
    // 'manual' was called 'paste' until Phase 5b; migrateReview renames it on load.
    // For a model-written review the kind is the provider id (Phase 5c).
    kind: 'claude' | 'openai' | 'manual' | 'file';
    provider?: 'claude' | 'openai'; // Phase 5c; withGeneratorProvider fills 'claude' on load when missing
    model?: string; // model that actually answered (modelUsage key, or the Responses API's model)
    effort?: string;
    guidelinesHash?: string;
    costUsd?: number; // Claude: SDK estimate. OpenAI: this app's price table, only for known models
    costEstimated?: boolean; // true when costUsd is the app's own arithmetic; the status bar says "estimate"
    usage?: unknown; // result.modelUsage or response.usage, stored verbatim
    durationMs?: number;
    sessionId?: string;
    paragraphBase?: 0 | 1; // Phase 5d: what the paragraph numbers in `raw` count from (5.2); absent on older reviews
  };
  raw?: unknown; // untouched structured_output from Claude, never edited
  hunks: Hunk[];
  comments: ReviewComment[];
}
```

Settings also carries `provider: 'claude' | 'openai'` (default `claude`), which the review dialog's provider select is bound to, and `openaiModel` (default `''`, meaning "the newest gpt-5 the key can see"). `model` stays the Claude model under its old name so every settings.json on disk still reads correctly; `modelFor(settings, provider)` picks the right one, so neither provider can be sent the other's model. `effort` is shared. The OpenAI key is not a setting and is never in settings.json (CLAUDE.md rule 2).

Invariants:

- Hunks never overlap. Sorted by `start`. Anchoring rejects the later of two overlapping hunks and records it as `stale` with a note in `reason`.
- An edit the author types is a hunk like any other (`source: 'manual'`), so the composed output stays a pure function of base plus decisions and every edit is reversible and re-anchorable. A hunk carrying `supersededBy` stays `stale` through a rebase: its words often still exist inside the edit that replaced them, and re-anchoring it there would undo a decision the author already made.
- `base.text` is the file content normalized to LF with BOM removed. All offsets refer to it. EOL, BOM, and trailing-newline flags are restored on write.
- YAML frontmatter at the top of the file (`---` ... `---`) is never inside any hunk and is never sent to Claude as an editable paragraph.
- The composed output text is a pure function of `base` and `hunks`. See `compose.ts`.

## 4. Text algorithms (`src/shared`)

### 4.1 Paragraphs (`text/paragraphs.ts`)

Split `base.text` on runs of blank lines (`/\n[ \t]*\n+/`), keeping exact `start`/`end` offsets for each paragraph and preserving the separators. A paragraph index is stable for the life of a review. Frontmatter is returned separately with `isFrontmatter: true` and excluded from numbering sent to Claude.

### 4.2 Compose (`diff/compose.ts`)

For each hunk in offset order, the desired side is `replacement` when `status === 'accepted'`, otherwise `original`. If `desiredSide !== baseSide`, splice the other text into the span. Because hunks do not overlap, a single left-to-right pass with an offset delta is enough. Restore EOL/BOM/trailing newline last.

### 4.3 Change list to hunks (`diff/anchor.ts`)

Input: Claude's `changes[]` and the paragraph list.

1. Look for `before` as an exact substring of paragraph `p`. One match: `exact`. Several matches: choose the first and mark `ambiguous`.
2. No match: try paragraphs `p-2..p+2` (Claude occasionally miscounts). Found once: `exact` with corrected paragraph index.
3. Still nothing: fuzzy match with diff-match-patch `match_main` against the whole text near the paragraph's start offset, `Match_Threshold` 0.3. Accept only if the matched span has small edit distance relative to `before` length (for example, Levenshtein ratio under 0.2). Mark `fuzzy` and set `original` to the actual matched text.
4. Otherwise `stale`. The hunk keeps `before`/`after` for display in the side panel.
5. Normalize: if `before` and `after` share a common prefix and suffix, trim both so the hunk covers only the differing words. This makes the floating suggestion short and precise.
6. Drop any hunk where trimmed `original === replacement`.
7. Resolve overlaps as in the invariants.

### 4.4 Full-text diff to hunks (`diff/wordDiff.ts`)

1. Split both texts into paragraphs. Align them with jsdiff `diffArrays` on paragraph strings (exact equality first; if a paragraph was edited it shows as remove+add).
2. For each removed/added paragraph pair in sequence (pair by position within the changed block; unpaired ones become whole-paragraph hunks), run `diffWordsWithSpace` and group consecutive changed tokens into one hunk: `original` = concatenated removed tokens, `replacement` = concatenated added tokens. Adjacent hunks separated only by whitespace are merged.
3. Offsets are computed in the original text. `source: 'diff'`, no category or reason.

### 4.5 Rebase (`diff/rebase.ts`)

When the file changes on disk (or before a Save when the disk hash differs from what the app last knew):

1. New base = disk text, normalized.
2. For every hunk, the text expected in the new base is `baseSide === 'original' ? original : replacement`. Also try the other side: if the user already applied the change by hand in Obsidian, the replacement is what exists now. Search near the old offset first (window of a few hundred characters), then the whole text, then fuzzy. Update `start`, `end`, `baseSide`, `anchor`. A side that holds no text, the empty side of a deletion or an insertion, is a zero-width place found by the text that surrounded the hunk (48 characters either side together, then 24 and 12, then either side alone), never inside a word, and it competes with the other side's words (`apply.23`, C.52). Candidates rank by word boundaries, then by how much of the 40 characters either side still reads as before (where both texts begin or end, the edge counts as reading as before), then the side the base holds, then distance. The other side wins, and the decision follows it (C.5), only where the file plainly shows the change made or undone there: an empty place whose surroundings read as before in full on one side, or for at least 12 characters of whole words on both (a side with less old text than that says nothing), once copies of the hunk's own words within 40 characters of it have been weighed against it in whole words (`settleCopies`, the `apply.23` review): a copy that reads further takes the place's claim away; words whose surroundings read as before on both sides, or on one side when they did not stand beside a copy of themselves (the twin of a doubled word); or, for a hunk whose own side has words, the only such words in the file. A decision Save would write stays only where its own side is plainly in place: words with one side of their surroundings as before, or the only such words when they did not stand beside a copy of themselves, and not tied with a place beside them; an empty place with one side as before in full and the other for at least 12 characters of whole words. Only words are placed by the fuzzy search: a deletion or an insertion with no place found exactly goes to Unanchored.
3. Hunks found nowhere become `stale` and drop out of the inline view, and so does a hunk whose other side seems to be in the file but not plainly, or whose unsaved decision would otherwise be written at a guessed place. A deletion or an insertion that is stale stays stale: only its surroundings could place it, and a stale hunk has none.
4. Comments keep their paragraph index if the paragraph text still exists (match by first 40 characters), otherwise `paragraph: null`.
   Property tests should confirm: compose(rebase(review, compose(review))) reproduces the same output, and status is preserved across a round trip.

### 4.6 EOL and BOM (`text/eol.ts`)

Detect `\r\n` presence, a leading UTF-8 BOM, and a trailing newline on read. Normalize to LF internally. Restore all three on write so git diffs and Obsidian see only the intended changes.

## 5. AI integration (`src/main/providers/`, `src/shared/prompt`, `src/shared/ai`)

### 5.0 The provider layer (Phase 5c)

Every service that can answer "Review with AI" implements one interface in `src/main/providers/types.ts`:

```ts
interface ReviewProvider {
  id: 'claude' | 'openai';
  label: string;
  runtime(): Promise<RuntimeStatus>; // can it run now, and under whose account or key
  models(): Promise<ModelInfo[]>; // in the order the dialog shows them
  review(request: ReviewRequest, hooks: { onProgress; onRateLimit?; signal }): Promise<ProviderResult>;
}
// ProviderResult: { output: ReviewOutput (the shared zod schema), raw, model, usage, costUsd?, costEstimated?, durationMs, ... }
```

- **IPC is provider-neutral.** `ai:runtime`, `ai:models`, `ai:review` and `ai:cancel` each name the provider in the payload; progress and rate-limit notices arrive as `ai:progress` and `ai:rate-limit`. The IPC layer (`src/main/ipc.ts`) owns request ids, Cancel (an `AbortSignal` per request) and the broadcasts; providers only call their hooks, so no provider imports IPC. The key has three channels of its own, `secrets:openai-status`, `secrets:openai-set` and `secrets:openai-clear`, and none of them ever returns the key.
- **Everything downstream is shared.** Both providers get the same system prompt (5.4) and the same numbered chapter (5.2), and both replies go through the same zod schema (5.3), the same anchoring, hunks and comments. A review records `generator.provider` and the model that answered; the status bar and the history badges show both.
- **Claude** (`providers/claude.ts`) is 5.1 and 5.6 unchanged, moved behind the interface.
- **OpenAI** (`providers/openai.ts`) uses the official `openai` package's Responses API:
  - `instructions` = the system prompt, `input` = the numbered chapter, `text.format` = `{type: 'json_schema', name: 'manuscript_review', strict: true, schema}`, `reasoning.effort` from the app's effort, `store: false`, `stream: true`, and the request's `AbortSignal`.
  - The schema is the draft-07 export rewritten by `toStrictJsonSchema` (`src/shared/ai/openaiReply.ts`) into the strict subset: every property required, `additionalProperties: false` everywhere, optional properties made nullable, and unsupported constraint keywords dropped. On the way back `stripOptionalNulls` removes exactly the nulls that transform introduced (a comment's `quote: null`), keeps legal ones (`paragraph: null`), and the shared zod schema validates the result. `raw` keeps what the model sent.
  - Streaming: `response.output_text.delta` accumulates the text and drives the progress label with an estimated token count (four characters per token) until `response.completed` supplies the real usage. A stream that ends without `response.completed`, or with `response.incomplete` or `response.failed`, is an error.
  - Models: `models.list()`, filtered by `isTextModel` (drops embeddings, speech, images, moderation, realtime, transcription, search and fine-tunes), newest first by `created`; the default is the newest id starting with `gpt-5`, else the newest text model. The list is fetched when the dialog first shows OpenAI, not at launch.
  - Effort: `OPENAI_EFFORT_FOR` maps low/medium/high/xhigh/max. The installed SDK's `ReasoningEffort` accepts all five, so the map is the identity (C.25); the dialog says that not every model accepts every level.
  - Errors: 401/403 -> "OpenAI rejected the API key" with an Open Settings button on the card; 429 -> "rate limiting this key, or the account is out of credit"; 5xx, connection failures and a garbled stream -> a retry hint; 400/404/422 -> the API's own message plus "the model or the effort level may not be available"; a reply that fails to parse -> a message naming the model that answered and what was wrong. Cancel maps to `cancelled` like Claude's.
  - Cost: token usage is always stored. USD is shown only when the model is in `src/shared/ai/pricing.ts` (standard-tier prices copied 2026-09-11), and then with the word "estimate". An unknown model gets no cost rather than a guessed one.
  - `MANUSCRIPT_REVIEW_OPENAI_BASE_URL` (formerly `STET_OPENAI_BASE_URL`), a development switch that packaged builds ignore (C.39), points the SDK at another base URL. Otherwise the client is always built with `https://api.openai.com/v1` and a null organization and project, so the SDK never takes them from `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, `OPENAI_ORG_ID` or `OPENAI_PROJECT_ID`. It exists so the path can be driven against `test/helpers/mockOpenAi.ts`; nothing sets it in normal use.
- **The key** (`src/main/secrets.ts`): Settings has a password field with Save key and Remove key. Main encrypts with `safeStorage.encryptString` and writes base64 ciphertext plus the last four characters to `<userData>/secrets.json`. If `safeStorage.isEncryptionAvailable()` is false, it refuses to store and Settings says why. `OPENAI_API_KEY` is used only when nothing is stored, and Settings and the dialog say which source is active. The key is decrypted only inside `providers/openai.ts`'s client factory, per call.

### 5.1 The request

```ts
import { query } from '@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk';

const q = query({
  prompt: renderChapter(paragraphs), // see 5.2
  options: {
    systemPrompt: buildSystemPrompt(guidelinesMd), // static instructions + GUIDELINES.md
    model: settings.model, // alias such as 'opus'
    effort: settings.effort, // 'low' | 'medium' | 'high' | 'xhigh' | 'max'
    outputFormat: { type: 'json_schema', schema: reviewOutputJsonSchema }, // draft-07
    maxTurns: 1, // raise if structured output needs more, see risks
    settingSources: [], // do not load the user's ~/.claude settings or CLAUDE.md
    persistSession: false,
    includePartialMessages: true, // progress from message_delta usage
    disallowedTools: ALL_BUILTIN_TOOLS, // see risks: verify the current tool names in sdk.d.ts
    cwd: app.getPath('userData'), // never the vault
    env: { ...process.env },
    abortController,
    stderr: (line) => log.debug(line)
  }
});
```

Message handling while iterating `q`:

- `stream_event` with `event.type === 'message_delta'`: read `usage.output_tokens` for a progress counter.
- `system` / `model_refusal_fallback`: note the fallback in the review's generator info.
- `system` / `model_refusal_no_fallback`: surface `api_refusal_explanation` to the user.
- `rate_limit_event`: show the subscription rate-limit info in the status bar.
- `assistant` with `error` set (`authentication_failed`, `rate_limit`, `billing_error`, ...): map to a user-facing message. `authentication_failed` opens the sign-in instructions.
- `result`: success requires `subtype === 'success'` and a present `structured_output`. Parse with zod. Any other outcome is an error shown with the SDK's message. Record `total_cost_usd`, `modelUsage`, `duration_ms`, `session_id`.
- The `for await` loop can throw after an error result. Wrap it.

Exported constants `USAGE_LIMIT_ERROR_PREFIXES`, `USAGE_WARNING_PREFIXES`, and `USAGE_TRANSITION_PREFIXES` from the SDK identify usage-limit text in results. Use them.

### 5.2 Chapter rendering (`prompt/chapter.ts`)

Send paragraphs numbered so the model can reference them. **Numbers start at 1** (Phase 5d), the same numbers the author sees in the rail beside each paragraph (7), so a comment that says "see paragraph 12" means the paragraph labelled 12:

```
<chapter file="Chapter 12.md" paragraphs="57">
[1] It was the third night of rain, and the river had began to climb the steps.

[2] "You should of told me," Mara said.
...
</chapter>
```

The app itself keeps 0-based indices everywhere (`Paragraph.index`, `Hunk.paragraph`, `ReviewComment.paragraph`). `paragraphNumber` and `paragraphIndexFromNumber` in `prompt/chapter.ts` are the only crossing between the two, and `fromPromptNumbering` converts a model's reply once, in the renderer, before `createAiReview` anchors it; a model that answers 0 anyway is clamped to the first paragraph. The system prompt tells the model the numbering and to cite paragraph numbers, never line numbers. Reviews record `generator.paragraphBase: 1`, which says what the numbers in `raw` count from; older model reviews, prompted from 0, have none. Their stored hunks and comments were always 0-based and display correctly; only the free text of an old comment ("see paragraph 37") still counts from 0.

Markdown is left exactly as written. Frontmatter is omitted. The parser strips nothing on the way back because Claude returns quotes, not the chapter.

### 5.3 Output schema (`prompt/schema.ts`)

```ts
export const ChangeSchema = z.object({
  paragraph: z.number().int().min(0),
  before: z.string().min(1), // exact text copied from that paragraph; shortest span that is unique in the paragraph
  after: z.string(), // '' means delete
  category: z.enum(CHANGE_CATEGORIES),
  reason: z.string().max(240)
});
export const CommentSchema = z.object({
  paragraph: z.number().int().min(0).nullable(),
  quote: z.string().max(200).optional(),
  category: z.enum(COMMENT_CATEGORIES),
  text: z.string().max(1200)
});
export const ReviewOutputSchema = z.object({
  changes: z.array(ChangeSchema),
  comments: z.array(CommentSchema)
});
export const reviewOutputJsonSchema = z.toJSONSchema(ReviewOutputSchema, { target: 'draft-7' });
```

Keep it shallow. The SDK validates against draft-07 and retries on mismatch.

### 5.4 System prompt (`prompt/system.ts`)

Static part written by us, then the contents of `GUIDELINES.md`. The static part states: you are a copyeditor for fiction; return only the structured output; `before` must be copied verbatim from the numbered paragraph, minimal but unique; never quote across paragraph boundaries; edits of more than about eight consecutive words are forbidden and must become comments; do not restate the chapter; if there is nothing to fix, return empty arrays. The guidelines file (see `resources/GUIDELINES.default.md`) carries the author-editable rules and style sheet. The same system prompt string for every chapter lets the SDK's automatic prompt caching apply.

### 5.5 The review dialog

"Review with AI" opens a modal rather than firing a request, because a review costs real money and minutes and the settings that shape it were sitting in the toolbar all day for a decision made a few times a day. The dialog asks, in one place:

- **Provider**, a select backed by `Settings.provider`: "Claude" or "OpenAI (API key)" (Claude's label was "Claude (subscription)" until `opensource.6`). Choosing OpenAI swaps the model list, adds an **API key** row (saved in Settings / from OPENAI_API_KEY / missing, with Open Settings), a line saying it is billed per token, and a note under Effort saying what reasoning effort is sent. Start review stays disabled until a key exists. Opening the dialog with OpenAI chosen and no key also raises a card with an Open Settings button; the Claude Setup screen only stands in front of the dialog when Claude is the provider.
- **Model** and **Effort**, persisted on change. The model select writes `model` or `openaiModel` depending on the provider.
- **Guidelines**, the effective path with an **Open** button (`guidelines:open`).
- **Chapter stats**: paragraphs and words, from `chapterStats` in `src/shared/text/stats.ts`, so the size of the job is visible before it is paid for.
- **The previous review**, if there is one: when it was made, how many suggestions it holds, how many are still pending, and a sentence saying that a new review replaces the working set while the old one stays in History.
- **Start review** and **Cancel**. Escape cancels.

The toolbar keeps only the button, the progress label and Cancel while a review runs.

### 5.6 Startup runtime check and setup screen

On every launch, `src/main/providers/claude.ts` runs `checkRuntime()` and reports one of `ready`, `missing-binary`, or `not-signed-in` to the renderer. The result is shown in the status bar and, when not `ready`, as a full Setup screen that is also reachable from Settings. Nothing is cached across launches; the check is cheap.

1. **Binary.** The app runs the user's own Claude Code; nothing of Anthropic's ships (C.42). `src/main/providers/claudeBinary.ts` (`setup.3`) looks, in order, where Anthropic's installer puts it (`%USERPROFILE%\.local\bin\claude.exe` on Windows, `~/.local/bin/claude` elsewhere), in each `PATH` folder, then in `/opt/homebrew/bin` and `/usr/local/bin` on macOS or `/usr/bin` and `/usr/local/bin` on Linux. On Windows only `claude.exe` counts, never npm's `claude.cmd` shim, which cannot run without a shell. Development and packaged builds look the same way. `--version` below `MIN_CLAUDE_CODE` is outdated, fixed by `claude update`; nothing found is `missing-binary`. The same module is the only one in `src` that imports `child_process`, and its `runClaudeCli` runs nothing but `--version` and `auth status --json`, without a shell or a window.
2. **Auth.** _Since `cli.1` (C.42): `claude --version`, `claude auth status --json` and the `initialize` exchange, which sends no prompt._ Formerly: run a minimal query (`prompt: 'ping'`, the tools-removed options from 5.1, `maxTurns: 1`) and call `q.accountInfo()`. An assistant message with `error: 'authentication_failed'`, or a thrown error naming authentication, means `not-signed-in`. Otherwise `ready`, carrying `email`, `subscriptionType`, and `apiKeySource` so the UI can say whether the subscription login or `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is in use (the key takes precedence when set).
3. **Setup screen content.** Exactly one of the two states below, with the detected OS tab preselected, a copy button on every command, a "Check again" button, and a link to https://code.claude.com/docs/en/setup.

   Missing binary, "Install Claude Code":
   - macOS, Linux, WSL: `curl -fsSL https://claude.ai/install.sh | bash`
   - Windows PowerShell: `irm https://claude.ai/install.ps1 | iex`
   - Windows CMD: `curl -fsSL https://claude.ai/install.cmd -o install.cmd && install.cmd && del install.cmd`
   - Alternatives: Homebrew `brew install --cask claude-code`, WinGet `winget install Anthropic.ClaudeCode`, npm `npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code`
   - Verify with `claude --version`, then sign in as below.

   Not signed in, "Sign in to Claude":
   - Open a terminal, run `claude`, and sign in with your Claude account in the browser. A Pro, Max, Team, Enterprise, or Console account is required; the free plan does not include Claude Code.
   - Alternatively set `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` in the environment before launching the app.
   - Then return here and click "Check again".

4. The app must not offer its own sign-in form, must not read or write `.credentials.json`, and must not run the runtime check with the vault as `cwd`.
5. Policy note for the README: this app runs the unmodified Claude Code each user installs and signs in to themselves, with their own subscription or API key (C.42). If the app is ever distributed, every user signs in with their own account through Anthropic's flow; the app never intermediates credentials.

## 6. Files, watcher, review store (`src/main`)

### 6.1 Files

- List `*.md` and `*.txt` recursively under the manuscript folder, skipping dot-folders and the reviews folder if nested. Both extensions are read, saved and reviewed the same way; the empty state says "No .md or .txt files in that folder".
- Read: bytes -> refuse anything over 4 MiB or not UTF-8 (`decodeText`, `files.3`, 11.3 l) -> detect BOM/EOL/trailing newline -> LF text -> sha256 of bytes. The refusal (`not-utf8`) says what the file most likely is (UTF-16, an older encoding, a few damaged characters, binary) and how to fix it, and ends "The app has not changed the file." Save re-reads through the same path, so a file swapped to another encoding after it was opened is refused rather than overwritten.
- Write: compose -> restore EOL/BOM -> write `<file>.<random>.tmp` in the same folder -> `rename` over the original. Remember the hash of what was written so the watcher can ignore the echo. A rename refused with EPERM, EACCES or EBUSY (a scanner, OneDrive or Obsidian holding the file for a moment) is retried after 50, 100, 200, 400 and 800 ms; before each retry the target is hashed again, and if someone else changed it the save stops as `stale` and their bytes stay (`files.4`). When the retries run out, `explainWriteError` says in plain words what Windows refused, including the Controlled folder access way out, and that nothing was written.
- **Main decides what Save may write (`files.2`).** `files:save` first runs `assertSavableChapter`: the file must be `.md` or `.txt`, must not be the guidelines file, and must lie inside the manuscript folder or have been read or created from inside it earlier in the run (`OpenedChapters`, so a chapter open while Settings changes folder still saves). Anything else is refused with `invalid-input` before a byte is read or written.
- **Create (`files:create`).** The New chapter dialog (7.5) creates a file in the manuscript folder through `createChapter`, which writes with the exclusive-create flag (`wx`): it can fail, but it can never overwrite. `checkChapterName` is the gate in front of it and refuses a path separator, a traversal, a reserved Windows device name, an illegal character and anything resolving outside the manuscript folder; `files:check-name` runs the same function plus an existence check, so the dialog can say "A file with this name already exists" while the author is still typing and keep Create disabled. A new file is written LF, no BOM, with a trailing newline; after that Save preserves whatever shape the file has.
- **The review folder name includes the extension** for anything but `.md` (`chapter-01` versus `chapter-01-txt`), so two files that differ only by extension cannot show each other's reviews. `.md` keeps the bare slug, which is what every review written before `.txt` support already uses.

### 6.2 Watcher

chokidar on the manuscript folder with `awaitWriteFinish: { stabilityThreshold: 300, pollInterval: 100 }`. On change to the open chapter: hash the file; if it equals the last written or last loaded hash, ignore; otherwise send `file:changed` to the renderer, which triggers rebase (4.5) and shows a banner: "Reloaded from disk. N suggestions re-anchored, M could not be found.", followed by "K decisions changed to match the file." when the rebase changed decisions to follow the file (C.5; `rebaseCardText` in `src/shared/ui/rebase.ts`, which words all four rebase cards: this one, a chapter opened on a review made before the file changed, an older review opened from History, and Save to chapter finding the file changed, `apply.23`). Obsidian saves on a debounce while typing, so this can fire repeatedly; that is fine because rebase is idempotent.

`createWatcher` (`src/main/watcher.ts`, `files.5`) takes chokidar, `broadcast`, the file reader and the log as dependencies, so it is tested with a stand-in and fake timers; `npm run test:slow` runs the real-chokidar timing tests in `test/slow/`, which `npm test` leaves out. Only .md and .txt files count: a change is reported unless its bytes are ones the app wrote or loaded (compared by `pathKey`), a chapter or folder appearing or disappearing refreshes the file list once per 300 ms burst, an added chapter is also hashed so an editor that saves by replacing the file still reloads the open chapter, and watcher errors are logged.

### 6.3 Save flow

`saveChapter` in `src/renderer/state/saveChapter.ts` (`apply.3`) is the one path from the page to the chapter file. The toolbar's Save to chapter, Ctrl+S, the review dialog's Save to chapter, and later the leave and close prompts all call it and can wait for how it ended: `saved` or `nothing-to-save` (the file now reads like the page), `cancelled` (Cancel to applying paragraph boxes), `blocked` (no chapter, a review of it running, or a box that cannot be applied) or `failed` (a card says why). It reaches main through `SaveDeps` that App passes in (`files:save`, the review writer, a refresh of History and the list), so it is tested in node. Saves run one at a time, and `pendingSave()` hands a caller that must let one end first the save under way.

0. Nothing unsaved (`unsavedDecisionCount` is 0 and no paragraph box has changed text): a card says "The chapter already matches the page." and the file is not read or written. Paragraph boxes with changed text are applied first as accepted suggestions, after a question unless the caller already asked (`applyDrafts: 'apply'`, for a prompt whose Save reads "Apply and save first"); if one cannot be applied, nothing is written. A box still as it opened needs no question and is closed when something is written, since the save can renumber the paragraphs under it.
1. Re-read the file and hash it.
2. If the hash differs from the review's `base.hash`, rebase first and tell the user what changed, then try once more.
3. Compose. When the bytes to write are the bytes already on disk, nothing is written (`SaveResponse.unchanged`, 11.3 h step 3), so the file keeps its modified time and Obsidian and sync tools see no change; the card then says the chapter already matches the page. Otherwise back up the bytes being replaced (6.5; no backup, no save), check once more that the file has not changed, and write atomically. Then update `base` to the written text (accepted hunks now have `baseSide: 'replacement'`, `savedReview`) and save the review. Comments and hunk paragraph labels follow their paragraphs into the new base (`remapAfterCommit`, C.44), so a save that splits, joins or deletes a paragraph does not shift them. While the file is being written the store holds back decisions (`chapterSaving`): the new base folds in exactly the decisions that were written, so a click in that moment does nothing rather than being recorded as saved without being in the file. Opening a chapter or a History entry waits for a save under way to end (`pendingSave()`), and of several clicks made meanwhile only the last goes ahead. If the review on screen is replaced during the write all the same, by a load that began before the save: a copy of the same review on the same base whose decisions compose the same text is folded like the review itself (`isWrittenReview`); another chapter or review is left alone, the save is recorded off screen in the review it belongs to, and History is refreshed only for the chapter on screen; a copy of the same review that reads differently, such as one rebased onto other bytes, is left alone too, and the card says the review was reloaded while saving and to save again.
4. Obsidian notices external changes and reloads the file when it is not being edited there. Document that the same chapter should not be edited in Obsidian while accepting changes; the app cannot prevent it, only detect it.

**Ctrl+S**, or Cmd+S, runs the same save wherever the focus is (`saveShortcutAction` in `src/shared/ui/save.ts`, handled first in `state/keyboard.ts`, before the early return for modified keys). It does nothing behind a dialog, a question, Settings or the Setup screen, on a held key's repeats, while a review of the chapter runs or a save is under way, or when there is nothing to save; from inside a paragraph box a card says to press Done first, and while the suggestion editor is open a card says to press that editor's Save first. Ctrl+Shift+S and Ctrl+Alt+S pass through.

### 6.4 Review store

`<reviewsFolder>/<chapter-slug>/<ISO-timestamp>.review.json`. The folder is named after the chapter's file name only, so same-named chapters in different folders share it; a review belongs to the chapter its `sourcePath` names, compared as the file system compares paths (`samePath`), never to whatever its folder suggests (files.1, C.45). The current review is the belonging one with the latest `decidedAt ?? createdAt`; History lists the rest in creation order. `raw` is never modified. A small `index.json` per chapter folder is not needed; list the directory, with summaries cached by modification time and size.

**Reviews follow their chapter.** When nothing belongs to a chapter that opens, the reviews of a chapter path that no longer exists are taken over if they plainly are this chapter's: the vault moved (the last two path segments match) or the chapter was renamed or moved in Obsidian (a review of exactly its current text, at least 500 characters). The whole history moves to the new folder with the new `sourcePath`, through a journal so a crash cannot split it, and a card names the old path (11.3 g). `saveReview` writes only a `.review.json` directly in the folder the review belongs in and holding no other chapter's review; any other path it is handed gets a new entry in the right folder instead.

**The reviews folder always resolves.** When Settings names no folder, reviews go to `<userData>/reviews`, created on demand (`resolveReviewsFolder` in `src/main/reviews.ts`). No review operation ever fails with "choose a reviews folder first": a generated review costs real money and minutes, and losing one because Settings was incomplete is the worst outcome the app can produce. Settings shows the folder actually in use, marks it as the default when it is, and offers Change and Show in folder.

**Two save points.** A review is written immediately after Claude answers, and again, debounced by 700 ms, after every decision. Both write through `reviews:save`; passing the known path overwrites that history entry rather than starting a new one. Each review has its own queue (`createKeyedWriter` in `src/renderer/state/persist.ts`, used by `reviewPersistence.ts`, `apply.1`): moving to another chapter or another History entry never cancels a write still waiting, one write per review is in flight at a time and the last one written is the newest, and opening a chapter first flushes what is waiting so it never reads a review file that is behind the screen. The status bar shows "Decisions kept HH:MM" (local time, 24-hour clock) after each write of the review on screen, and only for that review: the time is stored with the file it was written to and shown while that file is the one open (`reviewKeptAt`), so a chapter whose review has not been written yet, or another review, shows no time. The tooltip is "Your decisions are kept automatically in the review. The chapter file changes only when you press Save to chapter." (`keptPhrase`, `KEPT_TOOLTIP`, `apply.4`; it said "Review saved", which read as the chapter). A failure raises a notification card once per distinct message rather than once per keystroke.

**Writing the chapter is separate.** The toolbar button is called **Save to chapter** because it is the only thing in the app that touches the manuscript file: it composes the accepted changes and writes them atomically (6.3). Review json persistence needs no button. The button counts what the chapter file does not have yet (`unsavedDecisionCount`): "Save 3 changes to chapter", "Save 1 change to chapter", plain "Save to chapter" when the file already reads like the page; a dot in `--unsaved` before the chapter name and "3 not saved to chapter" in the status bar say the same (`saveButtonState`, `unsavedPhrase`, `apply.4`). There is no other unsaved flag: `store.dirty`, which also counted a decision made and turned back, is gone.

### 6.5 Backups

Before Save to chapter replaces a chapter file, the bytes it replaces are copied into the app's data folder (`apply.10`, 11.3 h, Appendix D question 10). The author may not use git, and the data folder is never synced or indexed. `writeChapter` in `src/main/files.ts` runs the copy as its `beforeReplace` hook, which main fills from `chapterBackupHooks` in `src/main/backups.ts`, after the file has been read and found to be the one the review is based on, and only when the save changes bytes: a save that would write what is already there writes and backs up nothing (6.3 step 3). **No backup, no save:** if the copy cannot be made, nothing is written and the card says "Could not back up the chapter before saving, so nothing was written:" and why, in plain words (no space left, a folder that cannot be written, and so on). After the copy the file is read once more, and if it changed meanwhile (Obsidian or a sync wrote it during the copy) the save stops as `stale` and rebases, as in 6.3, so bytes that are not in a backup are never replaced.

- **Where.** `<userData>/backups/<chapter slug>-<first 8 hex of sha256(pathKey)>/`, one folder per chapter file: the slug is the one the reviews folder uses (6.4), and the hash of the chapter's path key keeps two chapters with the same file name in different folders apart. A `chapter.json` holding `{sourcePath}` is written the first time and never replaced, so a person restoring by hand can tell which file a folder belongs to.
- **Never in the vault.** The backups root must lie outside the manuscript folder and outside the folder of the chapter being saved (`assertOutside`). That fails only when the manuscript folder contains the app's data folder, or a development userData override points into the vault, and the save is then refused with that reason, so no backup can be listed as a chapter, indexed by Obsidian or synced.
- **Names.** The local time of the save, then the chapter's file name: `2026-10-14 16.04.33 Chapter 12.md`, which opens in any editor as the chapter it copies and sorts by time. A second backup in the same second gets `-2` after the time, then `-3`. Each file is created exclusively, so no backup is ever overwritten, and holds the old bytes exactly: BOM, line endings and all. A copy that fails part way is removed. The names are made and read by `formatLocalStamp`, `backupFileName` and `parseBackupFileName` in `src/shared/backups.ts`.
- **Duplicates.** When the newest backup already holds exactly the bytes being replaced, as when the author put the old text back in Obsidian and saved again, no copy is added and that one stands for the save.
- **What is kept.** After every write, `afterWrite` prunes that chapter's folder: the newest 20 backups stay, and so does the oldest backup of each local calendar day from today back through the 29 days before; the rest are deleted (`backupsToPrune`). A name that is not a backup's, `chapter.json` and anything the author put there included, is never deleted. Pruning is best effort: the chapter is already written by then, so a failure is logged (`backups.prune-failed`) and the save stands.
- **The answer.** `SaveResponse.backup` gives the backup's `name` and whether this save made it (`created`), for the save card and Settings to point at (`apply.11`, which also records restoring a backup by hand). The log gets a line when a backup fails (`backups.failed`) or a prune does, and never a backup's contents (11.3 m).

## 7. UI specification (`src/renderer`)

Layout: left column file list with badges (has review, pending count) and a header carrying a folder button and a "+" for a new chapter; center review pane with a comment gutter; right column with the suggestions list, the focused suggestion's details, open comments, resolved comments, stale/unanchored suggestions, and review history below. The right column does not scroll as a whole: the suggestions list, its heading pinned, takes what it needs up to about 55% of the height and scrolls on its own, and everything below it scrolls as a second region, so the details stay in view while j and k step through the list; when the focus moves, a row already in view stays where it is, and the details come back into view if that region was scrolled away from them. In a window less than 540 CSS pixels high inside, which zoom changes and a chapter does not, the column scrolls as one instead, with the list at its full length: the smallest window, 900 by 600, from 110% zoom, and the default 1440 by 940 from 175% (C.49). Category filter chips sit under the toolbar. Status bar: a "?" legend, pending/accepted/dismissed counts, the count not saved to the chapter ("3 not saved to chapter", in `--unsaved`, only when there is one), the time the review on screen was last written ("Decisions kept 14:02", 6.4; nothing for a chapter whose review has not been written), provider and model used ("OpenAI · gpt-5-mini-..."), cost (with "estimate" when it is the app's own arithmetic), rate-limit notice, the active provider's readiness, and the zoom control. Its items never break in two; zoomed in, the bar wraps onto a second row instead.

**Zoom** (round 5). The whole window zooms, prose and controls together, from 80% to 200% in fixed steps (`shared/ui/zoom.ts`): the status bar's minus and plus, a click on the percentage to reset, Ctrl+plus / Ctrl+minus / Ctrl+0, and Ctrl+wheel. `src/main/zoom.ts` is the one place that decides it: it applies the level to the window, keeps it in settings (`zoom`), puts it back after every load, and tells the page (`view:zoom-changed`) so the label follows. The keys are caught in `before-input-event`, so they work wherever the focus is, and Ctrl+wheel arrives as Electron's `zoom-changed` event. The side columns narrow somewhat when the window is narrow in CSS pixels, which is what zooming in does, so the manuscript keeps most of the room.

**Blue buttons** darken on hover and press (`--accent-hover`, `--accent-active`) rather than taking the generic near-white hover, which had left their white text unreadable.

**Toolbar order**, left to right: the unsaved dot (only when something is not saved to the chapter) and the chapter title, "Review with AI" (progress and Cancel while it runs), "Submit manual review", "Bulk" with a dropdown holding Accept all, Dismiss all and Reset, "Save to chapter" with its count ("Save 12 changes to chapter", 6.4), "Copy result", "Settings". It has to fit a 1100 px window on one row, at 125% too while no review runs, which is why model and effort moved into the review dialog (5.5) and the three bulk buttons became one. The chapter title takes the room the buttons leave (there is no spacer) and is what gives way first: it ends in an ellipsis, with the full name in its tooltip, down to about 8em (`flex: 1 1 8em`). Past that, zoomed in or in the smallest window from 125%, the row wraps as the status bar does: the last buttons move to a second row on the right, and no button label ever breaks onto a second line (`apply.4`). While a review runs, its progress label (at least 15em, about 250 CSS px at "Writing suggestions (2345 tokens) (1m 12s)") and Cancel take about 200 CSS px more than Review with AI, so at 1100 px and 125% Copy result and Settings move to the second row until it ends. The label itself is free to wrap: the row wraps first, so it keeps one line, and if it is ever given less room than its words they break inside its own box rather than spilling over the name; with `white-space: nowrap` it had been drawn leftwards over the dot and the name, and cut off at the window's edge.

Messages, progress and confirmation:

- **Notification cards.** Messages appear as floating cards anchored to the top-right corner of the manuscript pane, sized to their text and stacked when several are open. A card closes only when its X is clicked: no click-away and no timeout, because a save error that vanished before it was read is worse than a stale card. Tones: info, warn, error. Cards about a recurring event (the watcher reloading the chapter, a save) pass a key, and a new one replaces the old, so a long typing session in Obsidian cannot bury the screen. Save errors use this path like everything else: every card that says how a Save to chapter ended, failures included, shares the key `SAVE_CARD`, so a save refused again and again (a backup that cannot be made, 6.5) leaves one card, and a save that then works replaces it with "Saved to the chapter file.". The card saying the chapter changed on disk and was rebased has no key and stays, because it counts the suggestions lost. Control glyphs are inline SVG rather than characters (see C.18).
- **Progress.** While Claude works the label cycles "Thinking", "Thinking.", "Thinking..", "Thinking..." every 500 ms with the elapsed time beside it, then switches to "Writing suggestions (N tokens)" once output tokens arrive. Cancel stays next to it. `src/shared/ui/progress.ts` owns the wording.
- **Confirmation.** "Accept all", "Dismiss all", "Reset" and the per-chip bulk actions open a small in-app modal, never `window.confirm`. It states the exact count and what is left alone: accept and dismiss only ever move pending suggestions, reset only clears decisions. Escape and Cancel back out; when nothing would change, a card says so instead of asking. `src/shared/ui/bulk.ts` computes both the affected ids and the wording. The same dialog asks the three-way questions before leaving a chapter or closing the window (11.3 i, `apply.6`): Cancel, the middle choice and the blue Save, left to right, in a dialog 460 px wide instead of 420, with the question's own default focused (Keep for later, or Cancel when only something would be lost). A question that cannot save hides Save, and an answer it does not show counts as Cancel. Labels never break inside a button; when the row does not fit, zoomed in, the last button moves to a second row on the right. `src/shared/ui/confirm.ts` decides the buttons and the focus. A question can be asked from inside another dialog or with the suggestion editor open, so the dialog sits above every other surface (`.modal-backdrop--confirm`, rendered last), takes Escape for itself, and shows the focus ring on its default however it was opened, since Enter answers that button.
- **Find.** Ctrl+F opens a find bar as a strip across the top of the manuscript pane, above the scroller, so it is in view wherever the author is in the chapter (C.33); pressing Ctrl+F again returns to the box with the query selected. It shows a match count ("3 of 12"); Enter and Shift+Enter or the arrows step, Escape closes. Matches are painted with the CSS Custom Highlight API over the existing text nodes, so hunk spans, ghosts and offsets are untouched (C.15). Ghost text and the paragraph rail are excluded from the search. The pane scrolls to the active match only when the author asks to go there, by typing or stepping (`find.seek` in the store); a redraw, such as accepting a suggestion with the bar open, repaints the matches without moving the page.
- **Scrolling.** Every scroll the app makes for the author (keyboard focus, a list click, a find match, a comment, a paragraph opened for editing) is animated by `state/scroll.ts` with the timing in `shared/ui/scroll.ts`: quick for a short hop, proportionally faster for a long one, and never more than 320 ms. A new scroll cancels the one in flight, so rapid j/k or Enter always lands on the last thing asked for. Reduced motion makes it instant. Every element that scrolls has an opaque background in a colour token, so it keeps a composited layer of its own with LCD text while it moves (C.48).

Review pane rendering:

- **Paragraph rail** (Phase 5d). Every body paragraph has, in a rail to its left, its number (from 1, matching the prompt, 5.2) and a faded pen that opens it for editing in place (7.4). A paragraph with an open comment has its number in the comment colour, where a dot used to be. The rail sits in padding added to the measure, is never selected or copied with the prose, and the find bar skips it. A block that spans two paragraphs (a deletion straddling the blank line) is labelled "12-13". Frontmatter has no rail.
- Prose font (Georgia or Literata with a serif fallback), `line-height: 2.3`, `white-space: pre-wrap`, max width about 70 characters. Text is rendered character-for-character from `base.text` so offsets stay valid; Markdown emphasis markers stay visible but are styled (italic for `*...*`, bold for `**...**`, headings larger). No Markdown-to-HTML conversion.
- Each hunk is an inline `<span class="hunk">` carrying its own `data-hunk-start` and `data-hunk-end`, with the live text in the line and a ghost row absolutely positioned above it (`top: -1.15em`, smaller font). The extra line height leaves room for the ghost. Ghost text is `user-select: none` so it can never join a selection.
  - **Colour means undecided.** Pending live text gets a 2 px underline in the category colour and a faint tint of it. Accepted gets a 2 px solid underline in the grey of the "was" ghost (`--ink-faint`) and no tint: the green underline it used to have read as "still active", which is the opposite of what it meant. Dismissed gets a 1 px dotted light-grey line. The hover tint follows the same rule, category-coloured only while pending. The "?" legend and the suggestions-list status markers say the same thing, and the strip beside the scrollbar marks pending hunks only.
  - **The dismiss "x" is part of the ghost row**, immediately after the ghost text with a 0.4em gap, so it trails the ghost whatever the two widths are. Pinned to the span's corner it landed on top of the ghost's last letters whenever the ghost was wider than the live text. The row counts as part of the suggestion for the pointer and reaches down to the line of text, a strip joins the top of the word to it, and the x keeps its room while invisible, so moving from the word to the x never loses it; the hover grace period covers a diagonal path to an x that sits past the word (C.34). Clicking the ghost toggles the suggestion, as clicking the word does.
  - **Ghosts that would collide are clipped.** Two suggestions close together on one line can have overlapping ghosts even when the words below them do not, and no CSS rule can know: it depends on where the line broke. `clipGhosts` in `src/renderer/state/ghosts.ts` measures the ghost rows after layout and gives any ghost that would run into the next one on the same line a max width and an ellipsis. The full text stays in the tooltip and in the suggestions list. Clipping changes no geometry the prose depends on, so it cannot loop.
  - The ghost is styled by **role, not position**, because the swap makes position meaningless: an original the author has replaced is always grey and struck through and is introduced by a small-caps "was"; a suggestion still on offer always carries the category colour and is introduced by an arrow. `ghostRole` in `src/shared/render.ts` decides which.
  - insertion (`original === ''`): live = a thin caret glyph; ghost = inserted text. Once accepted, the ghost reads "added", having no original to show (it used to read "was keep", which said the opposite).
  - deletion (`replacement === ''`): ghost shows a small "delete" tag.
  - A "?" popover in the status bar explains the three states in words, then the unsaved dot, and ends with the keys and pens (`e` rewrites the focused suggestion; the pen beside a paragraph edits it) and that nothing reaches the chapter before Save to chapter or Ctrl+S (`apply.4`). It scrolls when the window is too short for it.
  - A thin strip down the right edge of the scroller marks where the pending suggestions are; clicking a mark jumps to one.
- **Hover is not focus.** Hovering a suggestion opens a custom tooltip within 100 ms (category, reason, "Original: ... / Suggested: ...", and a pencil) and adds a hover tint. It never moves focus and never scrolls, because focus scrolls the pane and a suggestion that slid out from under the pointer is unusable. The tooltip is positioned below the words so it cannot cover the ghost. Only keyboard navigation and clicks in the suggestions list scroll the pane. Because the card now carries a control, leaving the words schedules its removal after a short grace period instead of doing it at once, and entering the card cancels that (`src/renderer/state/hover.ts`).
- Interactions: click the ghost or hunk = toggle accepted; the "x" beside the ghost, right-click, or the `d` key = toggle dismissed; `j`/`k` move focus; `a` accept; `u` back to pending; `e` edit the focused suggestion's text; the pen in the rail edits a paragraph; `n` next paragraph with a comment; Ctrl+S saves to the chapter, under the rules in 6.3. Bulk actions in the filter chips: accept all or dismiss all of a category, behind a confirmation. Everything is reversible until Save to chapter, and still reversible after it through rebase.
- **Suggestions list.** The right column lists every anchored hunk in document order, filtered by the category chips: a category dot, "original -> suggested" clipped to one line, a status marker, and edit, accept and dismiss icon buttons that act exactly as the pane does. A hunk the author wrote or rewrote shows a small "you" marker in place of the category dot. Clicking a row focuses that hunk and scrolls it to the centre of the pane; the focused row is highlighted and kept visible when focus moves by keyboard, by scrolling the list region only. Stale and unanchored hunks keep their own section below. The focused hunk's category, reason and both texts stay visible in a details block, with its own Edit button.
- **Comments as margin notes.** Each comment with a paragraph is a card in a gutter to the right of the prose, aligned with the top of its paragraph and pushed down when it would overlap the card above (`stackCards` in `src/shared/ui/stack.ts`). Clicking a card highlights its paragraph; resolve and "Edit text" live on the card. The sidebar list stays, and both views share one `activeCommentId`, so clicking either highlights the same paragraph. When the pane is too narrow for a gutter the cards fall back to the sidebar alone. Everywhere comments are listed (sidebar, margin, the `n` key) they are in reading order: by paragraph, ties in the order they came, chapter-wide comments last (`sortComments`).
- **Resolving a comment does not remove it.** A resolved comment stays in the margin as a greyed card collapsed to one line, its category and the first few words (`commentDigest`), and expands again when clicked. It is measured at a small height so it stays out of the open comments' way. In the sidebar it moves to a "Resolved comments" section below the open ones. Both views offer Unresolve, and both stay in sync as before. A resolved note that vanished read as a lost note.
- "Copy result" puts the composed text on the clipboard for pasting back into Obsidian.
- Settings dialog: manuscript folder, reviews folder (showing the effective path, whether it is the default, Change and Show in folder), guidelines file path (default `<manuscript>/GUIDELINES.md`, created from the template if missing, with Open guidelines file and Show in folder), Claude model, effort, Appearance (Match system, Light or Dark, saved through `settings:set`, with the note "Match system follows your computer's light or dark setting."; 11.3 d and e), Claude account status with the sign-in instructions from 5.6, and the OpenAI API key (status line, password field, Save key, Remove key, where to get one; 5.0). The shell actions go through `guidelines:open` and `shell:reveal`, which take a target name rather than a path, so the renderer can never ask the shell to open an arbitrary file.
- No application menu. `Menu.setApplicationMenu(null)` removes it entirely, so Alt does nothing over the manuscript. Development keeps DevTools (F12, Ctrl+Shift+I) and reload (Ctrl+R) through a `before-input-event` handler that exists only when the app is not packaged.

### 7.4 Editing inside the app

Accepting a suggestion used to be the only change the author could make without leaving for Obsidian. They can now write their own words, and **every edit goes through the hunk model**, so Save to chapter stays the only write path (CLAUDE.md rule 1) and an edit is as reversible and as re-anchorable as anything a model suggested. The rules are pure and tested in `src/shared/edit.ts`.

1. **Edit a suggestion.** A pencil in the tooltip, in the focused-suggestion details block, and on every row of the suggestions list (and the `e` key) opens a small floating editor prefilled with the suggestion's replacement: Save and Cancel, an "Accept now" checkbox, Ctrl+Enter and Escape. Saving updates `replacement`, sets `edited: true`, keeps source, category and reason, and accepts it unless "Accept now" is unticked. The new words are always a change against what the chapter file says over the span (`editSuggestion(base, ...)`, `apply.22`, C.51). A suggestion already saved into the file (`baseSide: 'replacement'`) takes the saved words as its `original` and its base side, and the new words as its replacement: ticked, it is accepted and counts as one unsaved change, and Save to chapter writes the new words; unticked, it is pending, the page keeps the saved words, and Save writes nothing for it rather than putting the model's original back. A saved change the author had turned back shows the saved words again the same way. New words that read exactly as the file does leave nothing unsaved: both sides become the file's words, and a suggestion whose two sides are the same never counts as unsaved and always shows the file's words, whatever is decided on it afterwards (`changesBase`, `decidedText`), so rewriting a saved suggestion and then back to the saved words is a no-op, and turning it back or dismissing it later still is.
2. **Edit a paragraph in place** (Phase 5d, replacing editing a mouse selection, which caught stray spaces and fought with clicking a word). The faded pen in the rail swaps the paragraph for a text box in the same typeface, prefilled with the paragraph as the page reads it, every accepted change applied. The box grows with its text; Done or Ctrl+Enter applies, Cancel or Escape discards. **Several paragraphs can be open at once**, each with its own Done. When a hunk straddles the blank line after a paragraph, the page already shows the two as one block, and the box takes the whole block. On Done, `planBlockEdit` word-diffs the page text against the box:
   - every changed run becomes its own accepted manual hunk (`source: 'manual'`, `category: 'other'`, `reason: 'Edited by author'`), so fixing two words far apart leaves two small hunks, not one that owns the paragraph; runs separated only by whitespace join, and each is trimmed to whole words, as the full-text diff does (4.4);
   - a run that touches a suggestion, overlapping it or merely adjacent to it, grows to take the suggestion whole: a model suggestion is superseded (dismissed, unanchored, `supersededBy`, reason kept behind "Superseded by your edit: "), and one of the author's own earlier edits is simply replaced, with anything it had superseded now pointing at its replacement;
   - typing exactly the fix a suggestion offered accepts that suggestion, keeping its category and reason;
   - typing the file's own words back needs no hunk: the suggestions in the way settle on the file's words (dismissed, or accepted when a save already wrote them in);
   - emptying the box deletes the paragraph with one blank line beside it, the one after if there is a next paragraph, as the full-text diff stores a deletion;
   - newlines at either end of the box are dropped; an unchanged box closes with nothing done.

   A property test checks that, for generated chapters, suggestions, saved states and edits, the composed chapter always equals the text before the paragraph, then exactly what was typed, then the text after, with no overlapping hunks.

   While a paragraph is open, its suggestions are frozen (their list rows fade and their buttons are disabled) and Bulk is disabled, because the box started from the page as it was and must commit against the same page. If the chapter file changes on disk while a box is open, the watcher still reloads the review and the box stays open; Done then refuses, says the paragraph changed, and keeps the author's text in the box. Opening another chapter or an older review with boxes open asks before discarding them. Save to chapter with boxes whose text changed asks to apply them first, as accepted suggestions, and saves nothing if any cannot be applied; boxes left as they opened are closed without a question when something is written (6.3). Review with AI and Submit manual review ask the author to finish or cancel them first.
3. **From a comment.** "Edit text" on every comment card, in the margin and in the sidebar, opens the comment's paragraph as in case 2, with the comment's quote selected when it can be found. This replaces the "Insert as suggestion" idea from the original Phase 5 list.

### 7.6 Read-only while a review runs (Phase 5d)

A review's result replaces the working set, so a decision made while it runs would vanish when it lands, and nothing used to stop the author making one. Now, while a review of the open chapter runs:

- the prose, the margin notes, the filter chips and the side panel are faded and `inert`, so no click, key or hover reaches them; the review-pane keys are ignored; Submit manual review, Bulk and Save to chapter are disabled; the store refuses every decision and edit for that chapter whatever the caller (`isLocked` in `state/store.ts`);
- a status card floats at the bottom of the manuscript pane, outside the scroller so it is always in view, with the progress label and Cancel review;
- other chapters stay open to read and edit. If the author has moved to another chapter when the result arrives, it is saved as that chapter's newest review and a card says so, instead of pulling the author back; opening the chapter shows it. If the chapter file changed on disk while the model worked, the result is rebased onto the text the chapter has now before it is shown.

The review dialog also warns when the page holds decisions the chapter file does not (`unsavedDecisionCount`): the model reads the file, and its result replaces the working set, so accepted changes that were never saved would drop out of view (they stay in History) and be suggested again. The warning carries a Save to chapter button.

### 7.5 New chapter, and manual reviews

Paste mode was one dialog doing two unrelated jobs. It is now two:

- **New chapter**, from a "+" beside the Chapters heading (the folder button next to it opens the manuscript-folder picker). File name, an extension radio (`.md` default, `.txt`), and a textarea for the text. The name is checked against the folder as it is typed and Create stays disabled while it is taken or unusable; the file is created through `files:create` (6.1), then the list refreshes and the new chapter opens.
- **Submit manual review**, from the toolbar. Paste a corrected version of the chapter that is open, or load it from a file, and the full-text diff (4.4) turns every difference into a suggestion. The review is saved to the reviews folder immediately, like a generated one, and carries `generator.kind: 'manual'`. When the open review already has decisions in it, a confirmation says that the working set is being replaced and that the old review stays in History.
