## 9. Risks and things the implementer must verify

1. **Structured output and `maxTurns`.** The Agent SDK may satisfy `outputFormat` through an internal tool call and retries. If `maxTurns: 1` prevents a valid result, raise it to 3 and keep tools removed.
2. **Removing tools.** `Options` has `allowedTools` and `disallowedTools`; check `node_modules/@anthropic-ai/claude-agent-sdk/sdk.d.ts` for a top-level `tools` option and for the current built-in tool names before hardcoding the list. The call must never be able to read or write the vault.
3. **SDK inside Electron main.** _Superseded by C.42: there is no SDK; the app spawns the user's `claude` itself._ The SDK spawns the bundled native binary, so Node on PATH is not required. Verify in `npm run dev`. If it spawns through the JS runtime instead, set `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` in `options.env` or pass `pathToClaudeCodeExecutable` pointing at a native `claude.exe`. Never point it at the npm `claude.cmd` shim.
4. **Packaging.** _Superseded by C.42: nothing of Anthropic's is packaged._ The native binary had to be unpacked from asar (`asarUnpack`).
5. **Model availability by plan.** Populate the model list from `q.supportedModels()`; do not assume `opus` exists on every plan.
6. **Refusal fallbacks.** Opus 5 can fall back to Opus 4.8 automatically when a safety classifier flags content, and some categories refuse with no fallback. Fiction with dark themes may trigger this occasionally. Show the explanation and record the actual model from `modelUsage`.
7. **Obsidian concurrent edits.** Detection is solid; prevention is not possible. Document the workflow.
8. **Policy.** The app is given away as open source and runs Claude Code. What keeps that within Anthropic's published terms as far as they can be read (sources and dates in Appendix A; Appendix D 8 and 21, C.42):
   - Each user installs, updates and signs in to their own, unmodified Claude Code, with Anthropic's installer and Anthropic's sign-in flow, on their own Claude plan or API key. Their usage is billed to them, and the app pays for, resells or intermediates none of it.
   - The app ships nothing of Anthropic's, neither binary nor library (`release.12` checks the package). It never reads, stores, displays or proxies Claude credentials. An `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` already in the user's environment reaches Claude Code only through the inherited environment, unread and unchanged (`buildClaudeEnv`). It never starts a sign-in; Setup only shows commands for the user to run (CLAUDE.md rule 2 (a)).
   - It never sets or overrides Claude Code's client or entrypoint identity. The Usage Policy that takes effect on 2026-11-12 bars services that "misrepresent the product or client being used". `buildClaudeEnv` adds one variable, `CLAUDE_CODE_STARTUP_FAILURE_RESULTS`, and `test/main/claudeOptions.test.ts` pins that (CLAUDE.md rule 6).
   - The author has not accepted the Commercial Terms (Appendix D 8). The legal page asks for them when Claude Code runs "in your products or services", and also says it does not prevent an end user signing in to the unmodified binary with their own subscription. No Anthropic page says which side of that line an app like this one falls on.
   - Anthropic may enforce its usage restrictions without prior notice, and acted against third-party tools in 2026 (Appendix A). If that happens to this app, Claude reviews may stop counting against the plan and be billed as extra usage, as third-party tools were in April 2026, or may fail. The OpenAI provider still works, and chapters and saved reviews are not affected.
   - The README carries `DISCLAIMER` (an independent project that runs Claude Code; not made, endorsed or sponsored by Anthropic or OpenAI; no Anthropic software) and `AI_OUTPUT_NOTICE`, both from `src/shared/branding.ts` and checked by `test/branding.test.ts`.
   - No Claude, Anthropic or OpenAI marks in the app's name or logo (`test/branding.test.ts` checks the name; the Seam logo has no lettering). Claude Code is named only in plain text, as the tool the app runs.

## 10. Guidelines file

`resources/GUIDELINES.default.md` is the author-editable "CLAUDE.md for edits". It is copied to `<manuscript>/GUIDELINES.md` on first use and sent verbatim after the static instructions. The author fills in the style sheet section (spelling variant, Oxford comma, character names, invented terms).

`ensureGuidelinesFile` creates it with `COPYFILE_EXCL`, so an existing file is never touched, and only a .md or .txt path is accepted. "Open" goes to Obsidian through `obsidian://open?path=` or to the system's app for .md, and when there is none shows the file in its folder and says so (`openGuidelinesFile`). The read-only `guidelines:status` reports `{path, exists, styleSheet}`, where `styleSheetStatus` calls the section `blank` while it holds only the template's labels and placeholders, `filled` once any entry has a value, and `missing` when there is no "## Style sheet" heading (`setup.10`).

## 11. Release 1.0: Lapidary

Written 2026-10-14 for implementing agents who were not part of the release discussion. It turns the 21 ideas agreed that day, plus one from the same day's round 6 feedback (small changes, 11.7), into milestones M0 to M12. Two answers the same day changed it: the name is Lapidary, not Igneous (Appendix D 1), and the app ships no Claude Code binary and the author accepts no Anthropic terms, so each user installs Claude Code themselves (Appendix D 8). Sections 0 to 10 still describe the app; a task that changes one of them edits that section in the same commit. Task ids such as `brand.4` and `apply.9` are stable: cite them in commit messages and tick them here. A task that absorbed others names them, and the absorbed ids are never done separately.

Rules for every task in this section:

- **Order.** Milestones run in order unless marked "can overlap". Inside a milestone, follow the listed order.
- **Hot files, one task at a time.** `src/main/index.ts`, `src/main/ipc.ts`, `src/shared/ipc.ts`, `src/main/providers/claude.ts`, `src/renderer/App.tsx`, `src/renderer/state/store.ts`, `src/renderer/components/ReviewDialog.tsx`, `src/renderer/components/SettingsDialog.tsx`, `src/renderer/styles/app.css`, `electron-builder.config.cjs` and `package.json` each receive edits from four to six workstreams. Tasks touching them run one after another, never as parallel agents.
- **Rule 7 and numbering.** New Appendix C entries take the next free number at commit time (C.38 was next on 2026-10-14) and are cited by title, never by a pre-assigned number. Once `release.6a` lands, every user-visible change also adds a line under `## [Unreleased]` in `CHANGELOG.md`.
- **Baseline.** On 2026-10-14 the working tree passed 364 tests in 26 files, typecheck, lint and format check. Counts only go up.
- **Safe verification.** Real-window runs use `npm run dev:clean` once `setup.15` exists; before that, `MANUSCRIPT_REVIEW_USER_DATA=.dev-userdata REMOTE_DEBUGGING_PORT=9222 npm run dev` (C.8, C.14). Saves go to a copy at `.dev-userdata/vault/`, never to the tracked `samples/`. Packaged runs use `dist/win-unpacked/Lapidary.exe --isolated-user-data=<scratch dir>` only. CDP key and wheel events skip Electron's input hooks (C.35); native close and file drags need real OS input.
- **Never, as an agent:** read `~/.claude/.credentials.json` or any `secrets.json`; run against the author's real settings; create Windows accounts, type credentials or sign in; click through SmartScreen; change OS or security settings; publish a release, change repo visibility or settings, force-push, or send a message. Those steps are marked **(author)**.
- **Commits.** Commit and push only as the author decided in `release.1`. Otherwise leave each milestone in the working tree with a proposed message.
- **Author sittings.** The author's hands-on steps are grouped: **A** (M0) decisions and the logo; **B** (M4) approvals to publish the repo; **C** (after M7) one UI session: theme contact sheet, scroll A/B, folder picker, the Obsidian save check, prompt wording; **D** (M11) the release session on a clean Windows account, then the 1.0.1 update proof.

### 11.0 Goal and scope

**Done** means a public, MIT-licensed repo `MichaelBradetLegris/lapidary` whose CI built and published `v1.0.0` as a GitHub Release, and on a clean Windows account: the friend downloads one installer, gets past the browser and SmartScreen warnings by following the README, installs Claude Code and signs in by following the Setup screen alone, reviews and saves a chapter, and receives `v1.0.1` through the in-app updater. The app is called Lapidary, has its own logo, dark mode, an unmistakable Save to chapter, a backup before every save, a close prompt, guards against encoding and Obsidian-syntax damage, a log file and Copy diagnostics.

**For** two people: the author (Windows 11, Claude Max) and a friend who writes novels in English in Obsidian, OS unknown. Windows first; macOS and Linux only if `files.17` says he needs them.

**Out of scope for 1.0, and cuts** (with the reason, so nobody rebuilds them):

- Automatic save to chapter: Save stays the one explicit write (CLAUDE.md rule 1), and background writes would race Obsidian's autosave and sync.
- In-app restore or Compare of a backup: restore is by hand; Compare is a follow-up that must never become the chapter's current review.
- Portable exe: re-extracts about 600 MB into `%TEMP%` per launch and cannot update. Local tests use `dist/win-unpacked`.
- Code signing unless Smart App Control blocks the friend (`release.14`, costs money). macOS and Linux builds unless he needs them.
- Electron fuses (from `hardening.9`), build-provenance attestation, a custom title bar, Windows forced colours, the version viewer (section 8 Phase 6).
- An in-app sign-in launcher: the legal page bars offering Claude login. Setup shows a command to copy.
- Bundling Claude Code (author decision, Appendix D 8): the installer contains no `claude.exe` and the author accepts no Anthropic terms. Each user installs Claude Code with Anthropic's own installer and signs in with their own account. The SDK's JavaScript library goes too (11.6 question 21): the app runs the user's `claude` command directly (11.3 q, `cli.1`).
- Cut tasks: `setup.3`'s `claudeExecutable` setting and newest-wins choice (untested SDK/CLI pairs); `setup.16` expiry warning (the CLI may never emit it on the SDK channel); `theme.11` (covered by `hardening.3a`); `hardening.14` (superseded by `files.4`); `release.15` (folded into `setup.3`); `hardening.7`'s domain allowlist, link-scan test, IPC sender check and permission handlers (no remote content; `opensource.12` covers navigation); `hardening.4`'s rescue plan (writers already flush); a second About surface; the `theme.3`/`theme.4` zero-pixel gate (the CSS test and contact sheet stay); `files.7`'s pair and line combination rules and subset property (the touching rule stays; a joint case needs two model edits that combine, visible in the ghost text first); `brand.8` and the old `release.1` repo rename and history rewrite (moot under a fresh repo); portable-specific Setup wording and smoke tests; a custom NSIS uninstall macro for the updater cache (documented instead).
- `apply.18` (a tracked Claude Code spawner) becomes measure-first: `sdk.mjs` spawns without `detached`, so libuv's kill-on-close job object should end `claude.exe` with the app. Build the tracker only if the measurement finds a leftover process.

### 11.1 Decisions

| Decision | Choice | Why |
| -------- | ------ | --- |
| Name | Lapidary, written only as `APP_NAME` in `src/shared/branding.ts`; a case-insensitive guard test enforces it | Lapidary work cuts and polishes stone, obsidian included, and lapidary prose is polished and concise. Igneous was dropped because two Markdown apps for Obsidian users took it in October 2026 (Appendix D 1). Renames stay one edit (C.21) |
| Frozen identifiers | `APP_ID` `dev.bradetlegris.manuscript-review`; package name `manuscript-review`; `APP_DATA_DIR` `ai-grammar-checker`; `APP_REPOSITORY` `MichaelBradetLegris/lapidary`. Never change after 1.0 | The NSIS uninstall GUID and AUMID derive from appId; the install folder `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\manuscript-review` and updater cache `%LOCALAPPDATA%\manuscript-review-updater` from the package name (`app-builder-lib` `targetUtil.js`, `appInfo.js`); every installed `app-update.yml` holds the slug. A package name unlike `APP_DATA_DIR` keeps `--delete-app-data` away from the data folder |
| Logo | Seam, the original pebble with its rim, ghost line, line of type and dotted underline, in the turquoise palette of 11.3 (o) (Appendix D 2); the ember palette is the alternative | No letterform, not purple, clearly unlike Obsidian's tall faceted crystal, visible on a dark taskbar |
| Repository | A fresh public `lapidary` repo, created when the author asks (Appendix D 3), one root commit by the GitHub noreply address; the private `ai-grammar-checker` is archived; branch `main`, remote `origin` | Old commits carry the gmail and a profile path, and `wip` does not build |
| Licence | MIT, "Copyright (c) 2026 Michael Bradet-Legris" | Short, compatible with everything shipped |
| Anthropic terms | None accepted (author decision, Appendix D 8). The app ships no Claude Code binary; each user installs Claude Code and signs in with their own account or key | The author will not bind himself to Anthropic's Commercial Terms for a free tool a handful of people use. Appendix A has the research. The SDK library is replaced by direct calls to the user's `claude` (11.3 q) |
| Claude wording | Provider label "Claude"; plain text says it runs the Claude Code the user installed, signed in with their own account or `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; `DISCLAIMER` and `AI_OUTPUT_NOTICE` in the README; no control starts a login | The legal page's section on products that run Claude Code allows a plain-text statement that a product runs Claude Code, and no Claude Code or Anthropic name or logo in its own name or logo; it also bars offering Claude login (Appendix A). The SDK branding guidance is for SDK integrations, which the app no longer is |
| Claude binary | The user's own installed Claude Code, run directly as a command (11.3 q); no Anthropic package is a dependency or ships. The installed version must be at least `MIN_CLAUDE_CODE`, the version the flags were verified against, else Setup says to run `claude update` | Matches the author's decisions (Appendix D 8 and 21). Claude Code updates itself, so new models need no app release. The author's own copy had stalled at 2.1.266 on 2026-10-14, so the version check matters |
| Launch check | `claude auth status` plus an SDK handshake that sends no prompt; failures from real runs outrank "ready" | Today it says ready when signed out and spends two model requests per launch |
| Default model | `default` for new settings, passed as no `--model` flag; a Pro account gets Sonnet preselected; existing `opus` kept; Pro accounts see a limits note; `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` removed from the child env | `default` is Opus 5.5 on Pro since CLI 2.1.280 (model-config docs), so the note does the real work |
| Dev switches | Read only in `src/main/devSwitches.ts`; dev builds only; packaged builds accept `--isolated-user-data=<dir>` | A shell-profile variable must never redirect the stored OpenAI key |
| Theme | `nativeTheme.themeSource` from a `theme` setting; CSS tokens swap under `prefers-color-scheme: dark`; no IPC | Electron 44 drives the media query and the Windows title bar (DWM attribute measured) live; the CSP forbids a pre-paint script |
| Scroll bolding | Opaque token backgrounds on every scroller (C.48) | Measured: only an opaque scroller gets its own LCD-text layer |
| Unsaved signal | `unsavedDecisionCount` on the button ("Save 3 changes to chapter"), status bar and a dot before the chapter name; `store.dirty` removed | One exact measure; "Review saved" read as "chapter saved" |
| Leaving and closing | Save / Keep for later / Cancel, Keep for later focused; a main-driven close handshake with 3 s and 10 s native fallbacks; shutdown never blocked | Nothing is discarded; a hung renderer cannot trap the window |
| Drafts | Open paragraph boxes persist under `<userData>/drafts` and come back, or are set aside as .txt | Updates, crashes and power loss skip every prompt |
| Backups | Before a Save that changes bytes, copy the old bytes to `<userData>/backups/`; keep newest 20 plus the first of each day for 30 days; no backup, no save | The friend may not use git; userData is never synced or indexed |
| Ctrl+S | Runs Save to chapter, except behind a modal, on repeat, in a paragraph box (card: press Done first), while the suggestion editor is open (card: press its Save first) or while locked | Reflex key; gating stops it cancelling prompts or saving without words still being typed |
| Encoding | Refuse non-UTF-8 files with advice per class; 4 MiB cap | Today bad bytes become U+FFFD, which Save would write |
| Obsidian syntax | App-owned prompt rule plus a pure guard that keeps out untouched model suggestions touching protected syntax (`keptOut`) | Deterministic and testable; compose unchanged |
| Reviews and files | A review belongs to its `sourcePath`; moved or renamed chapters adopt reviews by strict rules; `files:save` writes only chapters in the manuscript folder or opened this session | Today same-named chapters share reviews and Save can write the wrong file |
| Logging | Hand-rolled 1 MiB rotating log, scalar fields only, errors through one policy | No dependency; content cannot leak through structure |
| Installer | One-click per-user NSIS x64, `${productName}-Setup-${version}.exe`; `deleteAppDataOnUninstall` false | No UAC ever; it is exactly what the updater re-runs |
| Signing | Unsigned 1.0; Azure Artifact Signing only if Smart App Control blocks the friend (**about US$9.99/month, ongoing**) | SmartScreen warns at two-user scale even when signed; SignPath Foundation excludes proprietary components |
| Updates | electron-updater 6.8.10 and electron-builder 26.17.0 pinned (v26 line); GitHub provider, no token; check 15 s after launch and daily (toggle); download on consent; install only by Restart now with a visible installer; notify-only off Windows | Silent install-on-quit can half-install; 6.8.10 fixes a stale-blockmap bug |
| Versioning | SemVer from package.json; tags `vX.Y.Z`; 0.1.0 goes to 1.0.0; Keep a Changelog 1.1.0 | One number drives notes, About and the updater |
| CI | Check job on windows-latest and ubuntu-latest; Windows package job; draft release on `v*` tags with SHA-pinned actions and no cache | Free on a public repo |
| OpenAI | Labelled experimental; default never a -pro, -codex, -deep-research or -chat model; real-key check only if the author already has credit (**about US$5 top-up**) | Only ever tested against the mock |
| Telemetry | The app sends no analytics; Claude Code's own telemetry is documented with its opt-outs, not switched off | `DISABLE_TELEMETRY` also disables feature flags |
| Security | Single-instance lock, navigation and file-drop guard, CSP additions, IPC path guards; no fuses | Proportionate for a local app with no remote content |
| Electron | Latest 44.x before the cut (44.7.2 on 2026-10-14) | Current Chromium fixes for the first install |
| macOS, Linux | Conditional. macOS arm64 ad-hoc signed, notify-only updates; Apple Developer Program (**US$99/year**) only if the author wants auto-updates there | Notarization costs money; no Anthropic binary ships either way |

- [x] `files.21` **Comments and labels follow their paragraphs through a save** (done 2026-10-15; C.44) (reported by the author 2026-10-14). Pure and store code; may land any time after M2.
  - **Cause.** `markSaved` in `src/renderer/state/store.ts` folds the decisions into a new base with `commitDecisions`, which moves each hunk's offsets. It leaves every comment's `paragraph` and every hunk's `paragraph` label unchanged.
  - **Effect.** A save that adds, joins or deletes a paragraph shifts every later comment by one; the usual case is a split made with the paragraph pen. The author split 42-43 after a paste from LibreOffice Writer into Obsidian had lost line breaks. His comments marked 63 and 77 then quoted text that sits in 64 and 78. Changes made in Obsidian are not affected: `rebaseReview` already remaps comments, by the paragraph's first 40 characters and then by the quote.
  - **Fix.** A pure `remapAfterCommit(review, committed)` in `src/shared/review.ts` maps each old paragraph's start offset through the committed hunks. A comment follows its paragraph, or, when that paragraph was split, the part that contains its `quote`. Hunk `paragraph` labels are recomputed from their new offsets with `paragraphIndexAt`. `markSaved` uses it.
  - **Repair.** A load-time `repairCommentAnchors`, run by `upgradeLoadedReview`, moves a comment whose `quote` is missing from its paragraph to the nearest paragraph that contains it. Reviews already shifted, such as the author's current chapters, then correct themselves.
  - **Records.** PLAN 3 and 6.3; Appendix C "Comments follow their paragraphs through a save".
  - **Verify.** `test/shared/review.test.ts`: split, join and delete a paragraph through accepted manual hunks and save; every comment and hunk label then points at the paragraph holding its quote. A fast-check property over random paragraph splits and joins. The repair moves a shifted comment and leaves a correct one untouched. Real window: split a paragraph with the pen and Save; the margin notes further down stay beside their quotes.

Verify: `test/renderer/persist.test.ts` with fake timers; real window: accept then switch chapter in one CDP evaluation, and the first chapter's review JSON holds the decision.

- [x] `apply.22` **Rewriting a saved suggestion** (found by the `apply.2` review, 2026-10-16; older than M6). Rewriting the words of a suggestion that is already in the chapter file (accepted, then Save to chapter, so `baseSide` is `replacement`) changes the page but never the file: `editSuggestion` in `src/shared/edit.ts` sets `replacement`, `composeText` copies the base slice whenever the wanted side equals `baseSide`, and `unsavedDecisionCount` stays 0. Seen in a `dev:clean` window: "began" to "begun" accepted and saved, then `e`, "started", Ctrl+Enter: the card said "Suggestion rewritten and accepted.", the prose and the side panel showed "started", `unsavedDecisionCount` stayed 0, and a second Save to chapter said "Saved to the chapter file." with "begun" still in the file. When `baseSide` is `replacement`, treat the new words as a change against the base: for example the saved words become `original` with `baseSide: 'original'` over the same span, so compose writes the new words and the count shows 1, and rewriting them back to the saved words is a no-op. Decide and record what Accept now unticked does to a saved suggestion (today it turns it back to pending, so Save would put the model's original back). Verify: unit tests that edit a committed hunk, ticked and unticked, and check `composeText` and `unsavedDecisionCount`; the compose and rebase property round trips; the window steps above write "started". The fix is the one the text proposes: a saved suggestion takes the base slice, the file's words, as `original` with `baseSide: 'original'`, so compose writes the new words and the count shows 1. Decided for Accept now unticked: the rewrite goes to pending against the saved words, so the page reads as the file and Save writes nothing for it; the model's original is not put back. A saved change already turned back (pending or dismissed) is treated alike and shows the saved words again. Words equal to the file's make both sides the file's words with `baseSide` following the decision, which is what makes rewriting back a no-op, and also stops a never-saved suggestion rewritten to the file's own words counting as one unsaved change; a saved change turned back and rewritten to exactly its saved words keeps its `original`; a stale hunk only takes the new words. - [x] `apply.23` **Deletions through a rebase** (found by the `apply.3` review, 2026-10-16; older than M6). A deletion has an empty side, which `locate` in `src/shared/diff/rebase.ts` cannot find as text, so a rebase places it by the other side, and next to an identical word that goes wrong both ways. A saved deletion (accepted, then Save to chapter, so `baseSide: 'replacement'` and a zero-width span) comes back after any change on disk as a pending suggestion on another matching word: `locate` takes an `ambiguous` match of `original` and `rebaseReview` flips the hunk to pending there. Seen in a `dev:clean` window: "the the frayed" saved as "the frayed", then a line appended to the file as another editor would; the reload showed "→ DELETE ?" over "the" in "and the river", which accepting and saving would delete, and the card said "4 suggestions re-anchored, 0 could not be found" with nothing about the flip (`stats.adopted` is not shown). The other way round, an accepted deletion that is not saved but is already in the file (made by hand in Obsidian, or written by a save the page missed, as before the `apply.3` review fixes) is anchored on the remaining "the", whose surroundings still largely agree, and Save then deletes that one: seen in the same window, "and frayed end" written. Fix: in `locate`, place a hunk with an empty side by its surrounding context (`contextChars`) near the old offset as well, and prefer that zero-width place when its context agrees exactly; never flip an accepted hunk with `baseSide: 'replacement'` to pending on an `ambiguous` match of `original` (leave it, or make it stale). Count `adopted` in the save and reload cards. Verify: rebase property tests with a saved deletion next to an identical word plus a change elsewhere in the file (it stays saved, and the composed text keeps the remaining word), and with an accepted deletion of a doubled word already gone from the new text (adopted as saved, composing to the new text unchanged); the compose and rebase round trips; the window steps above. (done 2026-10-17; C.52, and 4.5 and 6.2 now describe it. `locate` in `src/shared/diff/rebase.ts` gives a side that holds no text zero-width places found by the old surrounding text (`placeCandidates`: 48 characters either side, then 24 and 12, then either side alone; never inside a word, `insideWord` in `src/shared/diff/fuzzy.ts`), scored together with the other side's words by `agreeBefore` and `agreeAfter`, where the start and end of the file count when both texts begin or end there. The place the word was taken out of scores 80 against its twin's 41 in the sample chapter, which is how "prefer that zero-width place when its context agrees exactly" is met. Differences from the text, and details it left open: the flip rule covers every flip in both directions, because insertions had the same fault (a suggested "the " was taken as made on any "the", and turning it back would have removed that word). The other side wins only where the file plainly shows the change there (`plainlyOnDisk`: a place found by its surroundings; words agreeing in full on both sides, or on one side when they did not stand beside a copy of themselves; or, with words on the hunk's own side, the only such words), a side counting only where the old file has text on it. A candidate that is not plain but ranks best makes the hunk stale (the text's "or make it stale"), and a match its surroundings set apart is not taken as ambiguous, so a deleted word put back where it was still comes back as a suggestion (C.5). Ties on context now go to the side the base holds before distance. Found while doing it and fixed here: an accepted deletion made by hand together with both neighbouring words was still placed on the twin, so a decision Save would write now stays only where its own side is plainly in place (`placedPlainly`: words with one side as before, or the only such words not beside a copy of themselves; an empty place with both sides as before), and never at a place found only by the fuzzy search; an accepted insertion made by hand with a word typed after it, or with the word before it changed, would have been written a second time, so an empty place found on one side only gives way to the other side's plain words; and a deletion or insertion already stale came back at the start of the chapter on the next rebase, its offsets being 0, so it now stays stale. Under Appendix D's friend defaults this is only: the author sends the release link, and the friend messages him on Discord or opens a GitHub issue if something goes wrong. The fuller trial, if the author ever wants it: set the manuscript folder to his chapters folder, fill and pin GUIDELINES.md in Settings, copy one chapter to a trial folder outside the vault, review, decide, edit, Save, compare (`fc.exe /n original.md trial.md` or a screen share with `git diff --no-index --word-diff`), switch back, check the style sheet is still in use, watch sync for two minutes. Record the outcome and any open `files.17` answers.

#### M12. Conditional (only when Appendix D says so)

- [ ] `release.14` **Azure Artifact Signing** (**about US$9.99/month**): if Smart App Control blocks the app or the author wants a publisher name. Author: pay-as-you-go subscription, identity validation, certificate profile, Entra app with the signer role, a protected `release` environment with the secrets. Config `win.azureSignOptions` only when the variables exist; `signExts` lists `ffmpeg.dll`, `dxcompiler.dll`, `vk_swiftshader.dll`, `vulkan-1.dll` (never a blanket `.dll`); the verifier with `EXPECT_SIGNED=1`. Once signed, every later release must be signed with the same publisher name.
- [ ] `release.16` and `brand.10` **macOS arm64**: dmg and zip, `identity: '-'`, `hardenedRuntime: false`, `signIgnore` for the Anthropic binary, notify-only updates; `icon-mac-1024.png` drawn on Apple's grid (about 824 px body, about 185 px radius); README "Open Anyway". Paid notarization path only with **US$99/year** approval.
- [ ] `release.17` **Linux x64** .deb and AppImage, `executableName: appName().toLowerCase()`, maintainer from the noreply address, AppImage auto-update, deb notify-only.
- [ ] `files.14` chunked reviews for multi-chapter files (more usage per review); `files.15` keep the creation time on Windows with a two-step rename and recovery; `files.18` follow a live rename of the open chapter.
- [ ] `hardening.11b` **OpenAI real-key check** (**about US$5 top-up**, only if the author already has credit): Appendix E checklist (model list and default, one review of Chapter 01, Cancel, cost estimate against the usage page, key removed); flip `OPENAI_MATURITY` to `tested` in the commit that records it.

### 11.3 Design notes

**(a) Startup order in `src/main/index.ts`.** Module scope: (1) `readDevSwitches({env: process.env, packaged: app.isPackaged, isolatedUserData: app.commandLine.getSwitchValue('isolated-user-data') or null})`; (2) `app.setPath('userData', switches.userDataDir ?? path.join(app.getPath('appData'), APP_DATA_DIR))`; (3) `if (!app.requestSingleInstanceLock()) app.quit(); else start();` and everything below runs inside `start()`, so a losing instance writes nothing; (4) `initLog(<userData>/logs)`; (5) `installCrashHandlers`; (6) packaged win32 only: `app.setAppUserModelId(APP_ID)`. `whenReady` (async): `Menu.setApplicationMenu(null)`; `registerCoreHandlers()` with every handler, including updates and the close guard (`handle()` only fills a Map and `installIpcBridge()` wires it once, so a later registration is silently dead); `installIpcBridge()`; `const settings = await loadSettings(); applyTheme(settings.theme); installTheme();`; `createWindow()` (themed `backgroundColor`, close guard, navigation guard, the one `render-process-gone` handler, window-open handler); `startUpdates()`. Events: `second-instance` restores and focuses; `before-quit` calls `closeGuard.onBeforeQuit()` and awaits nothing; `will-quit` calls `abortAll()` and `stopWatching()`; `window-all-closed` calls `app.quit()` off macOS.

**(b) Developer switches** (all read in `src/main/devSwitches.ts`; packaged builds ignore and log every one except the flag): `MANUSCRIPT_REVIEW_USER_DATA` (userData), `MANUSCRIPT_REVIEW_OPENAI_BASE_URL` (was `STET_OPENAI_BASE_URL`; http or https only), `MANUSCRIPT_REVIEW_DEV_NO_CLAUDE_CODE` (behave as if Claude Code were not installed; was `..._DEV_NO_BUNDLED_CLI` before C.42), `MANUSCRIPT_REVIEW_DEV_FAKE_UPDATE` (simulate an update to that version), `MREV_VERBOSE` (echo Claude Code stderr). `--isolated-user-data=<dir>` works in both builds and wins. The neutral prefix never changes with the name.

**(c) Claude child environment and options.** `buildClaudeEnv(base)` copies `base`, then removes `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE` (the switch behind `--bare`) and the switches in (b), and adds `CLAUDE_CODE_STARTUP_FAILURE_RESULTS = '1'`. It sets no SDK client variable, because the app is not the SDK (C.42). It never sets `DISABLE_AUTOUPDATER`: Claude Code is the user's own install and keeps itself current. Everything else passes unchanged, including `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_MODEL`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_*`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `DISABLE_TELEMETRY`, `DISABLE_ERROR_REPORTING` and proxy variables. No sign-in method is removed. `claudeArgs` (in `src/main/providers/claudeOptions.ts`, built on `BASE_CLAUDE_ARGS`) keeps the same guarantees as flags: no built-in tools (`--tools ""`), no MCP servers (`--strict-mcp-config`), no setting sources (`--setting-sources ""`), no session persistence, `--permission-mode dontAsk`, and `cwd` = userData (rule 6). Never `--bare`: it disables subscription sign-in. Because `settingSources` is empty, `apiKeyHelper` and env blocks in `~/.claude/settings.json` are not read; the README says to set the variable instead.

**(d) Settings.** Keys added in 1.0, all normalised in `src/shared/settings.ts` with defaults: `theme` (`system`; anything else not in system/light/dark becomes `system`), `onboardingDone` (missing: true when `manuscriptFolder` is set, else false), `dismissedHints` (`[]`, unique strings), `checkForUpdates` (true), `updateDownloadConsent` (null or a version string); `model` default becomes `default`, existing values kept. Read failures in `settingsStore.ts`: ENOENT gives cached defaults; a JSON parse failure first copies the file to `settings.json.corrupt-<yyyymmddhhmmss>` with `COPYFILE_EXCL`, logs `settings.unreadable`, then caches defaults; any other read error retries once after 200 ms, then returns defaults without caching, and `save` re-tries the read and refuses to overwrite.

**(e) Theme.** Main sets `nativeTheme.themeSource` before the window exists; CSS overrides every colour token inside `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) { :root { color-scheme: dark; ... } }`. The window is hidden until first paint, load or 3 s (C.36), and its `backgroundColor` comes from `WINDOW_BACKGROUND`, which the test pins to `--bg`, so no light frame shows. Light tokens added (values equal today's literals): `--accent-fill #3b5bdb`, `--on-accent #ffffff`, `--focus-bg #fff3bf`, `--para-active-bg #fff8dd`, `--para-flash-bg #fff6c9`, `--selected-bg #e7ecff`, `--badge-pending-bg #fff3bf`, `--badge-pending-border #f0dfa0`, `--badge-pending-ink #6b5600`, `--active-ring #f2dd93`, `--find-bg #ffe9a8`, `--find-active-bg #ffb64d`, `--find-active-ink var(--ink)`, `--warn-bg #fdf8ec`, `--warn-bg-strong #fdf7e2`, `--warn-border #e5d49a`, `--danger-bg #fdeceb`, `--editor-bg #fbfaff`, `--shadow-rgb 35 32 28` (shadows become `rgb(var(--shadow-rgb) / N%)`), `--backdrop rgb(35 32 28 / 42%)`, `--modal-shadow rgb(0 0 0 / 22%)`, `--hover-overlay rgb(0 0 0 / 8%)`, `--tint-pending 9%`, `--tint-hover 18%`, `--tint-decided-hover 16%`; editor glows become `color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent-fill) 12% or 24%, transparent)`. `--accent` is text, links and small marks (a commented paragraph's number, the information card's edge, the spinner, the open editor's border, the "you" chip; the unsaved dot was one until `apply.4` gave it `--unsaved`); `--accent-fill` is filled buttons (`button.primary`, the active Setup tab) and the editor glow, with `--on-accent` on them. Delete `--cat-typo`, `--cat-agreement`, `--cat-tense`, `--cat-redundancy`, `--cat-awkward`, `--cat-formatting`. Dark starting values: `--bg #1c1a17`, `--panel #24221e`, `--panel-alt #2d2a25`, `--border #3b3731`, `--border-strong #575148`, `--ink #ebe6dc`, `--ink-soft #bdb5a8`, `--ink-faint #968e82`, `--accent #9db1ff`, `--accent-fill #4263eb`, `--accent-hover #3653d6`, `--accent-active #2c45b8`, `--on-accent #ffffff`, `--accept #6cc28b`, `--dismiss #968e82`, `--danger #ff8a80`, `--warn #e0b955`, `--unsaved #ff8a3d` (added by `apply.4`), categories spelling `#ff7b72`, homophone `#f2875e`, grammar `#5cc0e0`, punctuation `#b994f5`, word-choice `#d9b44a`, consistency `#6cc28b`, other `#a8a092`, `--focus-bg #4a3f14`, `--para-active-bg #2f2a1a`, `--para-flash-bg #3d3415`, `--selected-bg #26304f`, `--badge-pending-bg #3d3415`, `--badge-pending-border #5c4f1f`, `--badge-pending-ink #e8c95a`, `--active-ring #8a7426`, `--find-bg #52420e` (#5e4b10 until `theme.5`, where `--ink-faint` on it, a find match in the frontmatter, measured 2.61:1; now 3.03), `--find-active-bg #e09a3a`, `--find-active-ink #16130f`, `--warn-bg #33291a`, `--warn-bg-strong #33291a`, `--warn-border #5c4a22`, `--danger-bg #3a1f1c`, `--editor-bg #20222b`, `--shadow-rgb 0 0 0`, `--backdrop rgb(0 0 0 / 60%)`, `--modal-shadow rgb(0 0 0 / 45%)`, `--hover-overlay rgb(255 255 255 / 10%)`, and the tint shares `--tint-pending 9%`, `--tint-hover 18%` and `--tint-decided-hover 16%`, the same as light, measured and kept by `theme.8` (below). `--font-ui` and `--font-prose` are the same in both themes and stay in the light block only; every other token is in both. Also: `a, a:visited { color: var(--accent) }`; zero-specificity `:where(select, input, textarea) { color: var(--ink) }` and `:where(input[type='password']) { background-color: var(--panel); border: 1px solid var(--border); border-radius: 4px; padding: 3px 6px }` (a background alone turns off the browser's drawing of the field, which falls back to a 2 px inset border, so it takes the other text fields' frame; no font, so `.modal__key-input` keeps its own); `accent-color: var(--accent-fill)`. Contrast tiers (WCAG 2.x, both themes): 4.5:1 for every text token and category on the three surfaces, categories on the active and flash paragraph backgrounds, `--ink` on every highlight, warn, danger and hover-tint background, `--ink-soft` and `--accent` on focus and selected rows, `--on-accent` on the fills; 3:1 for `--ink-faint` and `--dismiss` on surfaces and highlights and for categories as marks. The author approved the light tweaks (Appendix D question 9), so since `theme.5` `--ink-faint` and `--dismiss` are `#8a8378` and `--warn` and `--cat-word-choice` `#84681c`, and every light pair passes with no per-token floor (the floors that would have applied otherwise were warn and word-choice 4.2, ink-faint 2.5, dismiss 2.4). Every new colour goes into both blocks with its pairs. `--unsaved` (`apply.4`) is the ember of changes not saved to the chapter: light `#ad4b0d`, dark `#ff8a3d` (the ember of the logo's original palette, (o)), on the dot before the chapter name (a mark, 3:1) and the status bar's count (text, 4.5:1), both on `--panel`. Light measures 5.46:1 on `--panel`, 5.09 on `--bg`, 4.79 on `--panel-alt` and 4.71 on `--selected-bg`, dark 6.77, 7.40, 6.09 and 5.53, so `apply.5`'s dot in the chapter list passes on a hovered or selected row too. It replaces the `--accent` blue the dot had, so the dot no longer reads as a link or a comment. `test/renderer/themeCss.test.ts` pins every value in both blocks, so a changed value is edited there and here (dark) or on `theme.4`'s list of light differences (light) in the same commit. Surfaces for screenshots: Setup, nothing open, main view with a review covering every state, notification tones, find, paragraph editor, edit popover, lock card (by eye), every dialog, Bulk menu, legend and zoom.

Deliberate choices from `theme.8` (2026-10-16), after every surface was shot in both themes and the states screenshots miss were checked by hand:

- **Tints kept at 9%, 18% and 16% in dark.** Measured as the tinted box's WCAG contrast with what it sits on (the ratio counts screen flare, so dark differences are not overstated): a pending suggestion on the page is 1.14 to 1.18:1 in dark against 1.12 to 1.15 in light, hovered 1.35 to 1.45 against 1.26 to 1.34, and a decided one hovered 1.26 against 1.18. On the active paragraph dark is again the stronger, and on the focus yellow the two themes are within 0.04 of each other. The measure compares the range across the seven categories, lowest with lowest and highest with highest, and dark is at least light on every background. Category by category, spelling and homophone come out up to 0.03 lower in dark: spelling's pending tint is 1.145:1 on the dark page against 1.154 in light, and on `--focus-bg` 1.139 against 1.151 at rest and 1.302 against 1.334 hovered; homophone hovered on `--focus-bg` is 1.315 against 1.321. A gap that size is no reason to raise a share. At 100% and enlarged three times, every pending tint and hover in the main view is visible in dark. The tint is meant to be faint in both themes, as the 2 px category underline carries the state; raising it would make dark louder than light.
- **The focus ring is the browser's own; there is no `--focus-ring`.** Chromium draws `outline: auto` as a double ring, rgb(229, 151, 0) with a white edge in light and rgb(238, 238, 238) with an rgb(16, 16, 16) edge in dark, so it shows on any background. The white ring that `theme.5` and `theme.7` noted on the find field, the edit popover's box and a focused select is that dark ring. Every Tab stop checked showed a ring in both themes, the filter chips and zoom buttons included, whose `overflow: hidden` would clip a ring drawn outside them (Chromium draws it inside there). A colour of the app's own would be held to 3:1, which the light orange (2.36:1 on `--panel`) does not reach, so light would change too.
- **Text selection is the browser's own; there is no `--selection-bg` for now, and the author decides in sitting C (`theme.10` a).** White text on rgb(49, 101, 206) in light (5.4:1) and rgb(6, 57, 163) in dark (9.9:1), in the prose, the find field and the paragraph editor. Ghost text is never selected (`user-select: none`), but it sits in the gap between lines that the selection band covers, so while text is selected a ghost above a selected line is drawn on the band in its own colour. On the light band the categories read at 1.02 to 1.27:1 (word-choice 1.02, consistency 1.03, other 1.04, grammar 1.05, punctuation 1.07, spelling 1.21, homophone 1.27) and the struck-through "was" ghost (`--ink-faint`) at 1.44; on the dark band at 3.8 to 5.0 and 3.06. The "delete" tag is drawn at 80% and a dismissed ghost at 32%, so in the real window the red DELETE measured about 1.35:1 in light (2.9 in dark), and the dismissed "most of" in the selection shot about 1.1 (1.6 in dark), against 1.5 and 2.0 for the same ghost on the page. Kept for now because a selection is brief and the author's own act, the ghost is decoration that never joins a selection, and it reads as before once the selection is gone. The other way is a lighter band of the app's own, `::selection` on the prose only with `--ink` text (`--ink` on the browser's light blue is 3.0:1, so the band itself would change), in both blocks with contrast pairs for `--ink` and the `--cat-*` ghosts on it. That changes light, which needs the author's yes, so the contact sheet's selection shot puts the question to him.
- **Scrollbars are the browser's,** drawn from each block's `color-scheme`: thumb rgb(139, 139, 139) on rgb(252, 252, 252) in light, rgb(159, 159, 159) on rgb(44, 44, 44) in dark. The dark track is a neutral grey a shade cooler than the basalt panels. `scrollbar-color` would change the scrollbars' shape in light as well, so this waits for the author's report in sitting C (`theme.10` c).
- **Disabled buttons** keep `opacity: 0.45` in both themes, and read as disabled, the filled blue ones too (Save to chapter, Create, Start review).
- **Margin notes** have no connector lines; a note is tied to its paragraph by its ¶ number, the paragraph's number in `--accent` and, while active, `--active-ring` around the note and `--para-active-bg` behind the paragraph. All read in both themes. The notification cards and the lock card over the scroller read in both themes, with greyscale text as C.48 accepts. The lock card was looked at during a real review against the OpenAI mock's `slow` scenario.
- **A focused suggestion in the active comment's paragraph gets a hairline.** `--focus-bg` on `--para-active-bg` is 1.05:1 in light (1.37 in dark), and such a suggestion was hard to find, so `.para--active .hunk--focused` draws `0 0 0 1px var(--focus-bg), 0 0 0 2px var(--active-ring)` in place of the 2 px `--focus-bg` spread: the same 2 px, so it covers no more of the neighbouring letters, with its outer pixel in the active comment's ring colour (1.27:1 on the paragraph in light, 3.14 in dark). Both are told apart by hue more than by lightness (the focus yellow is 1.03:1 on the light page), so there is no 3:1 tier; `themeContrast.test.ts` checks that the ring stands out from the paragraph more than the yellow does, in both themes.

**(f) Claude status and the Setup screen.** Probes run in parallel and send no model request: `runClaudeCli(path, ['auth','status','--json'])` through `parseAuthStatus` (keeps only `loggedIn`, `authMethod`, `apiProvider`, `apiKeySource`, `subscriptionType`, `email`); the handshake (`query()` with a streaming prompt that never yields, `await q.initializationResult()`, always `q.close()`; 60 s budget on the first check, 20 s later); `--version` of both binaries. `auth status` is trusted only when it says signed out. `classifyClaude`, first match: `auth.loggedIn === false` gives not-signed-in; `account.apiKeySource` set and not `none` gives ready, api-key; `account.apiProvider` not `firstParty` gives ready, cloud; `account.email` gives ready, subscription; `account.tokenSource === 'none'` gives not-signed-in (when `auth.loggedIn` was true: "Claude Code is signed in through its own settings (<authMethod>), which <APP_NAME> does not load. Sign in with the command below instead."); no account: `auth.loggedIn` true gives ready with credential from `authMethod` (`claude.ai`, `oauth_token` subscription; `api_key`, `api_key_helper` api-key; `third_party` cloud), otherwise error. A free plan adds a plan warning. `classifyClaudeFailure`, first match (the caller handles refusal before): startup reason (`cli_version_too_old` outdated; `org_verify_failed`, `gateway_signin_required` login-expired; `org_pin_api_key_conflict` billing with API-key wording; `shell_tool_missing` "could not find PowerShell or Git Bash"); usage limit by the SDK's prefixes; text `/login expired|session expired|token (has )?expired|token revoked/i` login-expired; the assistant-error table (every row of today's `mapAssistantError`, plus `verification_required` billing, `cloud_credential_error` not-signed-in, `server_error` retry later); text rules (not logged in or please run /login; does not support this model or newer is required: outdated; invalid API key or disabled organization: billing with "An ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is in use and was refused"; credit balance or spend limit; not available with the plan; high load; overloaded, 529, 429); today's subtype table; else failed. A rewritten message keeps the original in `detail`. Views (`setupView`, first match): missing-binary gives not-installed; not-signed-in gives expired if the last failure is login-expired, else signed-out; a last failure of login-expired or not-signed-in outranks a ready runtime; error gives "could not check"; last failure outdated; usage limit in force; credential api-key; ready. The last failure clears on a successful review or connection test, on a signed-out check followed by a ready one, and (outdated only) when the binary path or version changes; a plain re-check never clears a sign-in failure. Review with AI opens Setup only for missing-binary or a definite not-signed-in; an error or timeout opens the dialog with a warning, and Setup offers "Try the review anyway". Commands: found at the default path or on PATH, `claude auth login` (or `& '<path>' auth login` on Windows and `'<path>' auth login` on POSIX when only the full path works, single quotes with `'` doubled); install-and-sign-in `irm https://claude.ai/install.ps1 | iex; & "$env:USERPROFILE\.local\bin\claude.exe" auth login` or `curl -fsSL https://claude.ai/install.sh | bash && ~/.local/bin/claude auth login`; outdated gives `claude update`. Signed-out intro: "Claude Code is not signed in. If reviews worked before, your sign-in has ended; this happens from time to time and nothing in <APP_NAME> is lost." Footer: "<APP_NAME> never sees your Claude password or sign-in token. You sign in with Anthropic, in Claude Code and your browser." Missing-binary is the first-run state for most people: it leads with the install-and-sign-in command, says Claude Code is Anthropic's free command-line tool that needs a Claude plan or an API key, and that <APP_NAME> does not include it.

**(g) Reviews bound to chapter files.** Invariant: a review file lives in `folderFor(reviewsFolder, review.sourcePath)`. Belonging: `samePath(review.sourcePath, chapterPath, ci)`. Current review: the belonging review with the greatest `decidedAt ?? createdAt`; History stays in `createdAt` order. `saveReview` honours an explicit path only if it ends `.review.json` and lies in the right folder; otherwise it writes a new entry there and returns its path (never throws for a path reason, so a reviews-folder change cannot break saves). Adoption, only when nothing belongs and the candidate's source file no longer exists: (a) at least 2 matching trailing path segments in the same slug folder (moved vault), or (b) equal `base.hash` with at least 500 characters (renamed or moved in Obsidian); rank hash matches, then more segments; two old paths equal on both adopt nothing, since it cannot be told which one the chapter was. Adoption rewrites every history file of the old path into the new folder with the new `sourcePath` and removes each old file only after its copy is written, following a journal (`.adoption.json` in the reviews folder) that the next store call completes if a crash interrupts it; an info card names the old path.

**(h) Save path and backups.** `writeChapter` order: (1) read the raw bytes once, refuse non-UTF-8 (`files.3`), hash; (2) hash differs from `expectedHash`: `stale`; (3) new bytes equal disk bytes: `written`, `unchanged`, no backup; (4) `beforeReplace`: refuse a backups root inside the manuscript folder, then write the backup unless identical to the newest; a failure aborts with "Could not back up the chapter before saving, so nothing was written: <reason>"; (5) re-read, and a changed hash returns `stale`; (6) `atomicWriteFile`: temp file in the same folder, rename, on EPERM, EACCES or EBUSY back off 50, 100, 200, 400, 800 ms, re-hashing the target before each retry (`changed` returns `stale`, `unreadable` skips a round), then `explainWriteError`; (7) remember the hash for the watcher; (8) `afterWrite` prunes backups, best effort. Layout: `<userData>/backups/<chapterSlug>-<first 8 hex of sha256(pathKey)>/` with a one-time `chapter.json` `{sourcePath}`, files named by local time, for example `2026-10-14 16.04.33 Chapter 12.md`, `-2` after the time on a collision. Retention keeps the newest 20 plus the oldest of each local day within 30 days and never deletes a name it cannot parse.

**(i) Leaving and closing.** `CloseGuardState = {chapter, unsavedDecisions, changedParagraphs, locked, reviewRunning, writesPending, dialogText}`, pushed on change. Main `createCloseGuard(deps)`: on window `close`, an allowed close, a gone renderer or nothing at risk closes at once with no IPC; otherwise preventDefault, reveal (restore, show, focus, flash), send `app:close-requested` once and arm a 3 s ack timer; no `received` opens a native "not responding" box (Wait / Close anyway); `received` arms a 10 s timer to a "still finishing" box; `prompting` clears both; a repeated close before `prompting` opens the box at once; every box has an AbortSignal so a late answer dismisses it; Close anyway destroys the window. `app:close-decision` true allows and closes on the next tick (and quits when quitting); false resets `quitting`. `query-session-end` and `session-end` only broadcast `app:flush`; shutdown is never blocked. `allowNextClose('update')` lets the update quit skip everything. Renderer `prepareToLeaveApp(reason)`: send `received`; race the in-flight save and both writer flushes against 5 s (on timeout or failure: "Still writing your decisions" with Close anyway and Wait); `closePrompt` (title "Close <APP_NAME>?"; buttons "Save N change(s) and close" or "Apply, save and close", "Close, keep for later" or "Close anyway", Cancel; Keep for later focused; lines say decisions are kept in the review and the chapter changes only on Save, open paragraphs come back, a running review stops, typed dialog text is lost, or that the decisions sit in an older History review from <date>); Save proceeds only on `saved` or `nothing-to-save`; Keep for later cancels a running review and flushes; a `finally` always answers. Leaving a chapter uses `leavePrompt` with the same three choices ("Save N change(s) first", Keep for later, Cancel), never prompts while locked, and re-clicking the open chapter only flushes.

**(j) Drafts.** `<userData>/drafts/<first 16 hex of sha256(pathKey)>.json` holds `{version: 1, sourcePath, reviewId, savedAt, drafts: [{key, paragraphs, initial, text}]}` (at most 200 drafts, 1 MB text each), written by a keyed 700 ms writer, flushed on leave, close and session end; an empty set deletes the file. On open, a draft reopens only when its paragraph still reads exactly `initial`; a changed draft that cannot be placed is written to `drafts/set-aside/<chapter> <stamp>.txt` and a card offers Show.

**(k) Protected Obsidian syntax.** Hard spans: frontmatter; fenced code (3 or more backticks or tildes) and inline code; `%%...%%`, `<!-- -->`, `$$...$$` across lines (an unclosed one protects only its opening marker); embeds `![[...]]` whole; `[[target]]` whole, and `[[target|alias]]` as `[[target|` plus `]]` so the alias stays editable; links as `[` plus `](url)`; images whole; footnote refs `[^x]`, line-start `[^x]:`, inline footnotes `^[` and their `]`; block ids `^id` at line end; tags `#` at line start or after whitespace or `(` with at least one non-digit; callout `[!type]` with optional `+` or `-`; line-start `>` and heading `#` prefixes; thematic-break lines; bare http(s) URLs; HTML tags only for real element names (a, abbr, b, br, blockquote, center, code, del, details, div, em, font, hr, i, iframe, img, ins, kbd, li, mark, ol, p, pre, s, small, source, span, strong, sub, summary, sup, table, td, th, tr, u, ul, video, audio) or with attribute syntax. Not protected: emphasis, bold, strikethrough, highlight markers, indented code, inline `$math$`, tables, lists. Guarded hunks: `source 'claude'`, not authored, pending, `baseSide 'original'`, not stale, no `keptOut`. First failing rule wins: before `bodyStart` gives `frontmatter`; not inside one body paragraph gives `paragraph-break`; overlapping a hard span (a zero-length hunk strictly inside one) gives that kind; `original` or `replacement` containing `[ ] % $ ^ # < > | ~`, a backtick or a newline gives `markup`; applied alone, changing the paragraph's `syntaxSignature` or paragraph count gives the kind or `markup`. After `resolveOverlaps`, a survivor starting exactly where the previous survivor on the line ends gives `combined`. A failing hunk becomes `anchor 'stale'`, `start = end = 0`, `keptOut: reason`; the note reads "Kept out of the text: it would change Obsidian syntax (<label>). Make this change in Obsidian or with the paragraph pen if you want it."

**(l) Encoding.** `classifyBytes` order: FF FE or FE FF prefix gives utf16; any NUL: utf16 when NULs fill at least 25% of the even or odd positions in the first 4096 bytes, else binary; valid UTF-8 (BOM allowed) passes; more valid multi-byte sequences than invalid bytes gives mixed (with the first bad line); else legacy. Messages: UTF-16 and legacy say how to re-save as UTF-8 (Notepad on Windows, VS Code elsewhere); mixed says to retype the N damaged characters in Obsidian and never re-save in Notepad; all end "The app has not changed the file." Decoding stays `toString('utf8')` so the BOM survives.

**(m) Logging policy.** Lines are `<ISO> <LEVEL> <event> k=v`, scalar fields only, strings scrubbed (OpenAI and Anthropic key shapes, Bearer tokens, emails, the home directory in plain, forward-slash, percent-encoded and `file:///` forms) and cut at 300 characters. `loggableErrorFields`: `*-invalid-output` and `claude-refusal` log the code only; other app errors log code and message; `SyntaxError` logs its name and stack frames only (V8 quotes the input); errno errors log name, errno, syscall; others name, message, frames. Never logged: chapter text, prompts, guidelines, replies, `detail`, the account email, the key's last4, drafts, backups.

**(n) Release and update workflow.** Builder: `publish: [{provider: 'github', owner, repo, releaseType: 'draft'}]`; `nsis: {oneClick: true, perMachine: false, runAfterFinish: true, createDesktopShortcut: true, shortcutName, artifactName: '${productName}-Setup-${version}.${ext}', differentialPackage: true, deleteAppDataOnUninstall: false}`; `win.target` nsis x64 only. `verify-package.ts`: asar listing has `/out/main/index.js` and `/package.json` and no `/samples`, `/test`, `/src`, `/dist`, `/.dev-userdata` (normalise backslashes); no `@anthropic-ai` package and no `claude.exe` or `claude` binary anywhere in the package; `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.txt`, `LICENSE.txt`, `GUIDELINES.default.md` exist; `--installer` adds `app-update.yml` (repo, no `publisherName` unless `EXPECT_SIGNED`) and `latest.yml` (version, file name, size). `release.yml`: on tags `v[0-9]+.[0-9]+.[0-9]+*`, windows-latest, `contents: write`, actions pinned by commit SHA, setup-node with `package-manager-cache: false` and no restored caches, the tag passed as `env: TAG` and never interpolated into scripts; steps `node scripts/check-release.cjs "$TAG"` before `npm ci`, lint, test, `npm run package`, verifier with `--installer`, `sha256sum` of the three files into `SHA256SUMS.txt`, notes from the CHANGELOG section plus footer, `gh release create "$TAG" --draft --verify-tag` with exactly 4 assets (`--prerelease` when the version has `-`). The author publishes with `gh release edit vX.Y.Z --draft=false --latest`; drafts are invisible to the updater. Updater flags: `autoDownload false`, `autoInstallOnAppQuit false`, `autoRunAppAfterInstall true`, `allowPrerelease false`, `allowDowngrade false`, logger = the app log. A consented version that reappears downloads from the cache without asking. Restart now: the renderer refuses while a review runs ("Restart after the review finishes"), runs `prepareToLeaveApp('update')`, shows the overlay, invokes `update:install`; main `runUpdateInstall` calls `closeGuard.allowNextClose('update')`, `abortAll()`, `await writesSettled(2000)`, `stopWatching()`, `autoUpdater.quitAndInstall(false, true)` and awaits nothing after it (the NSIS installer stops every process under `$INSTDIR` about 1.3 s after it starts). An install error resets the guard and shows the error card.

**(o) Logo concepts.** Shared geometry: `pebble_points(cx, cy, a, b_top, b_bottom, n, rot)` is a superellipse, x = a·sgn(cos t)·abs(cos t)^(2/n), y = b·sgn(sin t)·abs(sin t)^(2/n) with b = b_bottom where sin t > 0 and b_top otherwise, rotated by rot about the centre; vertical gradients via `Image.linear_gradient('L')` and `ImageOps.colorize` through a polygon mask; values are fractions of the side, drawn at 8x and reduced with LANCZOS; the small spec applies at 40 px and below. **A "Seam"** (chosen 2026-10-14, in turquoise: rim #5ad3db to #178e9c, body #29393c to #121b1d, ghost #7d999b, line #f1efe6, dots #4fd8e0; the colours below are the ember original, kept as the alternative): pebble cx .5, cy .52, a .46, b_top .42, b_bottom .36, n 2.6, rotation -5 degrees; rim = whole pebble #f08a3c to #c2401c, inner pebble (each semi-axis minus max(.035, 1.25/size)) #4a3d36 to #211a17; large: ghost #9a8577 x .33 to .67, top .32, height .05, fully rounded; line #f4ecdc x .25 to .75, top .43, height .11, radius height/4; 5 dots #ff8a3d diameter .07, gap .045, centred, top .62; small: no ghost, line top .40 height .13, 3 dots diameter .10 gap .07 top .60. **B "Ember tile"**: rounded square pad .03 radius .22, #ec7a3a to #a8361a; ghost #ffd2ad x .315 to .70 top .33 height .055; line #2a211d x .215 to .785 top .52 height .115 with serif uprights height .168 width .03; 6 dots #fff1e0 diameter .05 gap .04 top .688; small: line top .42 height .14, no serifs or ghost, 3 dots diameter .10 gap .07 top .64. **C "Cursor"**: pebble and rim as A with cy .53, b_top .41; line #f4ecdc x .20 to .54 centred at y .465 height .11 (small .14); ghost (large) #9a8577 x .26 to .50 top .30 height .05; ember #ff8a3d I-beam at x .66, stem width .075 (small .10) from y .27 to .69 (small .26 to .70), caps width .19 (small .22) height .065 (small .09), all rounded. None uses purple, facets or a radial spark. B sits closest to Claude's orange; C's I-beam reads as a letter. On macOS 26 any non-squircle icon sits on a grey tile, which `brand.10` handles.

**(p) Where data lives** (README and `opensource.11`). `%APPDATA%\ai-grammar-checker` (name predates the rename): `settings.json`, `settings.json.corrupt-*`, `secrets.json` (the encrypted OpenAI key), `reviews\` (default reviews folder, holds chapter text), `backups\` (full chapter copies), `drafts\` and `drafts\set-aside\` (typed prose), `logs\main.log` and `main.1.log`. `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\manuscript-review`: the app. `%LOCALAPPDATA%\manuscript-review-updater`: a cached copy of the installer, used for small updates. Uninstalling removes only the app folder; the README says how to delete the rest. The manuscript folder holds chapters and `GUIDELINES.md` (created once, never overwritten). The Claude sign-in belongs to Claude Code, never to the app. On macOS and Linux the data folder is `~/Library/Application Support/ai-grammar-checker` or `~/.config/ai-grammar-checker`.

**(q) Claude through the user's `claude` command** (11.6 question 21, `cli.1`). No Anthropic package is a dependency, so nothing of Anthropic's ships. `claudeCli.ts` runs the path from `setup.3` with no shell, `windowsHide`, `cwd` = userData, the env from `buildClaudeEnv`, and `-p --input-format stream-json --output-format stream-json --verbose --tools "" --setting-sources= --strict-mcp-config --no-session-persistence --permission-mode dontAsk --effort <e> --max-turns 3 --include-partial-messages`, plus `--model <m>` unless the model is `default`. The system prompt and the JSON schema travel in the `initialize` control request (`systemPrompt: [text]`, `jsonSchema`), as the SDK sent them, so a long guidelines file never meets the Windows command-line limit; the chapter goes in as one user message on stdin. It reads NDJSON lines: `system` init, `assistant`, `rate_limit_event`, and `result` with `structured_output`, `usage`, `total_cost_usd`, `modelUsage`, `is_error` and `subtype`. The same classifiers as before map failures. Cancel ends the process tree (`taskkill /PID <pid> /T /F` on Windows). The model list and account details come from the CLI's `initialize` control request over stream-json, which is what the SDK sent. Check it against the installed version first; if that exchange is unavailable, fall back to a static alias list (Default, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku) without version labels. Every flag was present in Claude Code 2.1.266's `--help` on 2026-10-14: `--tools ""` disables all tools, and `--bare` must never be used because it turns off subscription sign-in. `MIN_CLAUDE_CODE` in `src/shared/ai/claudeStatus.ts` is the oldest version the argument test was verified against.

### 11.4 Release checklist

Before the cut (agent unless marked):

1. M0 to M10 ticked or recorded as cut in this section; every M12 task marked build or cut in Appendix D.
2. `release.21` done; `npm view electron-builder dist-tags` and `npm view electron-updater dist-tags` checked for newer v26 patches.
3. The package verifier confirms that no Anthropic binary ships (Appendix D 8), and the pages in Appendix A were re-read for changes.
4. CHANGELOG `[1.0.0]` dated; README final; `npm run check:public` clean.
5. On a fresh clone of `main`: `npm ci`, `branding:check`, `check:public`, `lint`, `format:check`, `test`, `build`, `package` (verifier with `--installer`).

Cut:

6. `npm version 1.0.0 -m "Release %s"`; **(author)** approves `git push origin main` then `git push origin v1.0.0` (never `--tags` or `--all`).
7. The workflow creates a draft with exactly 4 assets; check `SHA256SUMS.txt` against the files.

Smoke test, sitting D **(author)**, on a new local standard account (Settings > Accounts > Other users > "Add a user without a Microsoft account") with no Claude Code, no `.claude` folder and no Node; record this PC's Smart App Control state:

8. Download the setup exe from the draft in Edge; screenshot every browser prompt; `Get-FileHash` matches `SHA256SUMS.txt`.
9. Run it; screenshot SmartScreen; expect only a progress bar, no UAC, install under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\manuscript-review`, shortcuts named Lapidary, a legible taskbar icon in light and dark.
10. About shows 1.0.0; Setup says Claude Code is not installed; the theme follows Windows; a second shortcut launch focuses the first window.
11. Install Claude Code and sign in using only the Setup screen (`setup.14`); the app turns Connected on refocus; record the CLI's printed lines; Test the connection works.
12. Copy `samples/manuscript` to Documents, complete the Welcome steps, run a review with the Default model, accept, dismiss, edit a paragraph, close with an open box (prompt), Save to chapter (backup created; Notepad shows the file with its line endings), Copy diagnostics (the agent checks the pasted text for versions and no secrets).
13. With the dev switch variables set in the environment, the app ignores them.
14. Uninstall: the app folder goes, the data folders stay; reinstall 1.0.0. Launch once with Appearance set to Dark: no light flash.
15. **(author)** Publish: `gh release edit v1.0.0 --draft=false --latest`. Check for updates now says "Up to date".

Updater proof: ship a small real fix as 1.0.1 through steps 4 to 7 and publish it. In the test account, 1.0.0 offers 1.0.1 within 15 s with a size; the download shows progress; Restart now with an open box raises the close prompt; after closing the box the overlay and the NSIS progress window appear and the app reopens as 1.0.1 with no UAC or SmartScreen; settings and reviews intact; Restart now during a review is refused; a downloaded but uninstalled update survives a restart without a second download. The agent reads "Full: X, To download: Y" from the pasted log and records sizes in PLAN; above about 20 MB for an app-only change, note `differentialPackage: 'store-asar'` (26.17.0) as a follow-up. Then the author runs `brand.9`'s carry-over checks on his own account, sends the friend the release link, and `files.13` begins.

Conditional on the friend's OS: **macOS** (`release.16`, `brand.10`): a `macos` job uploads dmg, zip, blockmaps and `latest-mac.yml`, a final job rewrites `SHA256SUMS.txt`; on his Mac: Safari download, Open Anyway, Setup ready, one review and Save, binary hash equals the manifest's, `codesign -dv` still shows Anthropic's team, the update card links to the release page. **Linux** (`release.17`): a `linux` job uploads .deb, .AppImage and `latest-linux.yml`; on Ubuntu 24.04 the deb installs, a review and Save work, and an AppImage updates itself.

### 11.5 Risks

- **Anthropic's terms move.** The author accepts no Anthropic terms and ships no Anthropic binary; each user runs their own Claude Code under their own agreement with Anthropic. The SDK documentation still discourages third-party products built on subscription sign-in, and Anthropic has pressed at least one free tool to drop it (Appendix A). If that happens here, the API-key route and OpenAI remain. Mitigations: no login control, no credential handling, plain-text attribution, the pages re-read before each release.
- **Claude Code version skew.** Each user's Claude Code updates on its own schedule; the author's had stalled at 2.1.266 on 2026-10-14. Setup flags anything older than `MIN_CLAUDE_CODE`. A newer Claude Code could rename or drop a flag the app passes; the pinned argument test, the per-release check in `release.10` and a clear error (never a silent wrong answer) cover that.
- **Smart App Control or S mode** blocks an unsigned app outright. Ask early (`files.17`); signing costs money and does not remove SmartScreen at this scale.
- **The NSIS updater kills the app** about 1.3 s after it starts. Guard, flushes, overlay and `allowNextClose` happen before the installer is spawned; drafts and decisions are on disk within 700 ms.
- **A close-guard bug traps the window.** Clean states close with no IPC, the renderer always answers in `finally`, two native fallbacks end in `destroy()`, and a gone renderer is bypassed.
- **Content leaks** through new log calls, diagnostics, issues or screenshots. The scalar-only API, one error policy, content-scan tests, `check:public` and the issue-form checkbox guard against it; screenshots come from the mock.
- **Supply chain.** Whoever controls the GitHub account or the release job can update every install; unsigned updates are checked only by sha512. 2FA, SHA-pinned actions, no cache, draft-then-publish.
- **SDK and toolchain churn.** The Agent SDK ships almost daily and CI cannot run a live Claude call; v26 fixes hide under the `v26` dist-tag; electron-builder 27 and electron-updater 7 are alpha. Quarterly Dependabot, a manual bump routine, exact pins.
- **Update size.** Without Claude Code the installer is mostly Electron (measure it in `release.4`); an app fix downloads most of the asar; deleting a published `.blockmap` turns every update into a full download.
- **Guard false positives** keep a few legitimate prose fixes out (a stray `#word`, "per cent" to `%`); they stay visible under Unanchored. Joint syntax creation by two model edits is possible after the trim and shows in the ghost text before acceptance.
- **Adoption** can attach a review from a disconnected drive to a same-named chapter; strict rules, a 500-character floor, no adoption on ties and an info card limit it.
- **Hot-file collisions** across workstreams: the one-task-at-a-time rule and the M2 seams.
- **The Linux CI leg** has never run; it stays non-required until it passes once.
- **The scroll fix may not be the whole story** (GPU, display); an "unchanged" A/B result is recorded as a known cosmetic issue.
- **Same-name products.** Igneous was dropped for this (Appendix D 1). On 2026-10-14 Lapidary had only small, unrelated software uses and no software trademark; the domain lapidary.app is someone else's.
- **Disk use**: the installed app plus the updater cache (measure both in `release.4`), which uninstall leaves behind (documented).

Sent as one message in `release.1`; answers go to Appendix D. All were answered or settled on 2026-10-14.

2. Logo. **Answered 2026-10-14: Seam in turquoise** (Appendix D).

21. The Claude SDK library. Without `claude.exe`, the app still ships Anthropic's Agent SDK JavaScript ("all rights reserved; use is subject to" Anthropic's legal page). Replace it with direct calls to the user's installed `claude` command (`claude -p` with `--output-format`, `--json-schema`, `--tools`, `--setting-sources`, `--strict-mcp-config`, `--no-session-persistence`, `--system-prompt`, `--model` and `--effort`, all present in Claude Code 2.1.266), so the installer contains no Anthropic code at all (recommended, a contained rewrite of `providers/claude.ts` plus CLAUDE.md's "only through the Agent SDK" line), or keep the SDK?
22. Colour. **Answered 2026-10-14: turquoise for now** (Appendix D).

### 11.7 Small changes you can see (`small`)

A change that touches only punctuation, a space or a one-letter word gets trimmed to 0 or 1 characters (4.3, step 5). Its mark in the line is then only as wide as that one character: 4 to 7 px of underline, or a 2 px caret for an insertion. In the screenshot the author sent with the round 6 feedback (2026-10-14), paragraph 74's accepted hyphen-to-space in "double standard" is a 4 px grey line under a space, and the faint "WAS -" above it has a strike-through that merges into the hyphen. Paragraph 76's pending comma after "Mira" is a 2 px purple bar with a 3 px comma above it and an invisible x 23 px to its right. Both are hard to spot while scrolling and hard to hit: about 5 x 22 px on the line, against 68 x 21 px for the word "standard".

**Decisions.**

- **Drawn bigger, never stored bigger.** Nothing here changes a hunk field, an offset, a text node in the prose, or anything `compose.ts` reads, so Save to chapter and Copy result cannot change (CLAUDE.md rules 4 and 5). Widening the stored hunk instead would move offsets, demote neighbours in `resolveOverlaps`, and let `touches()` supersede it more often. It would also make the ghost repeat the whole word, against 4.3 step 5.
- **One definition of small.** `isSmallText(text)` lives in a new `src/shared/ui/small.ts`. After combining marks (`\p{M}`) are removed, the text is small when it has at most 1 code point, or at most 3 code points with no letter or digit (`\p{L}`, `\p{N}`). So "", ",", " ", "i", "--" and "..." are small; "an", "it" and "----" are not. `trim.ts` already widens every letter change to its whole word, so this catches exactly punctuation, whitespace and one-letter words, which are all under one em at 17 px. Each surface asks the question of the text it shows:
  - the line asks `isSmallLive`, which tests the live face and excludes text holding a line break, because that box is split across lines;
  - the keycap tests the ghost face;
  - margin marks and list rows ask `isSmallChange`, which tests both sides, so a row looks the same whatever its status.
- **The caret stays with its word and follows status.** `.hunk__caret` becomes `display: inline`. The inline-block caret broke away from "Mira" onto the next line at 3 of 651 window widths, the inline one at none, and no word joiner is needed. The caret turns `--ink-faint` when accepted and `--border-strong` when dismissed. Today it keeps the category colour, against "colour means undecided" (7).
- **A capsule under small live text.** An absolutely positioned `::after` on `.hunk__live`, 0.75em wide or the glyph's width if wider. It is painted beneath the glyphs (`.para { isolation: isolate }`, `z-index: -1`), so neighbouring letters stay pure ink.
  - Pending: the category colour at `--tint-small`, mixed over transparent so it also sits correctly on the active paragraph's yellow, with a 2 px category `border-bottom` across it.
  - Accepted: a 2 px `--ink-faint` border-bottom and no fill.
  - Dismissed: a 1 px dotted `--border-strong` line.
  - Hit zone: a transparent `::before`, 0.9em wide.
  - Touching hunks: both pseudo-elements pull back to the hunk's own edge on any side where the neighbouring plain text is shorter than 2 characters, so neither covers a touching hunk.
  - Why it moves nothing: absolute boxes add no advance, so line breaks, the span rect (scroll target, tooltip, popover), the inputs to `clipGhosts` and the heights of margin notes are unchanged.
  - Borders rather than box-shadow, because forced colours drop shadows.
  - The click target on the line grows from about 5 x 22 to 16 x 23 px.
  - When focused, the capsule and `.hunk__ghost` (not the whole row) take `--focus-bg`.
- **A keycap ghost.** A small ghost sits in a box: a 1 px rounded border in the ghost's colour at 60%, no fill, and the glyph at 1.25 times the ghost size.
  - Whitespace is named in small caps: SPACE, 2 SPACES, NO-BREAK SPACE, TAB, LINE BREAK, PARAGRAPH BREAK.
  - An accepted insertion reads "ADDED [,]" instead of a bare "ADDED".
  - Text decoration does not reach inside an inline-block, so a small original is not struck through. Its "was" label carries that meaning, and the rule in 7 that a replaced original is always struck through gets this exception.
  - Case 1 then reads "WAS [-]". An inserted space reads "-> [SPACE]" instead of an arrow pointing at nothing. On case 2 the hover-only x moves from +23 to +38 px, so a click aimed at the comma no longer dismisses it.
  - No fill, because light `--cat-word-choice` is exactly 4.5:1 on `--bg` and any tint behind it would fail the theme contrast test.
  - A pure `ghostContent(hunk)` in `src/shared/render.ts` chooses the role label, tag and key, replacing the inline choice in `HunkSpan.tsx`.
- **Words for invisible characters.** One table in `small.ts` (`whitespaceName`, `characterName`, `sideText`) feeds the list row, the tooltip, the details and stale pairs, and the aria-label.
  - Case 1's row reads "- (hyphen) -> (space)" instead of "- -> (nothing)", which described it as a deletion. "(nothing)" stays for the empty string only.
  - Words, not U+2423 or U+00B6: a new reader will not know those symbols, and Literata may lack them.
  - List rows for small changes also show a few words on either side, with the change in a chip styled like the keycap ("...up with Mira [-> ,] who had..."), built by a pure `changeInContext(base, hunk)`. Stale rows keep their pair.
- **Margin marks for pending small changes, and an arrowhead for focus.**
  - A 3 px x 0.9em bar in the category colour sits in the rail, level with each line that holds a pending small change. An ink arrowhead marks the line where the focused hunk starts, whatever its size.
  - The left edge is where the eye returns on every line, and it is where proofreaders and change bars put their marks.
  - Marks are buttons with no text, placed by CSS anchor positioning (`anchor-name` on `.hunk__live`, `top: calc(anchor(top) - 0.53em)`). That follows reflow, zoom and font loading with no measuring pass. Electron 44.3.0 ships Chromium 152, where a mock placed marks within 0.5 px, including next to a caret that had wrapped to the next line.
  - The 11 x 39 px target lies outside the prose. Hovering shows the tooltip under the change, for reading only; to edit, click the bar and press `e`. Clicking selects without scrolling, and then `a`, `d` and `u` act.
  - Decided small changes get no mark, so a worked chapter goes quiet.
  - Bars and arrowhead are drawn with borders. The rail narrows from 42 to 39 px.
- **Keys that start where the author is reading.**
  - Shift+J and Shift+K go to the next and previous pending change, skip decided ones, and wrap.
  - When nothing is focused, or the focused change is off screen, `j`/`k` (and their Shift forms) start from the paragraphs on screen; otherwise they step as today. Today the first `j` after scrolling jumps back to the old focus or to paragraph 1.
  - Today Caps Lock also disables `j`/`k`, because only lowercase is handled. Shift is now read from `event.shiftKey`.
