The cards that report a rebase (a chapter opened on a review made before the file changed, an older review from History, the reload after a change on disk, and Save to chapter finding the file changed) end with "K decisions changed to match the file." when `stats.adopted` is not 0 (`rebaseCardText` in `src/shared/ui/rebase.ts`); they read as before otherwise. What counts did not change: only a decision that changed, so an accepted deletion found already made is not counted, being accepted before and after.

What this gives up. A hunk the old code placed by its best guess now leaves the page, to Unanchored, when the file around it changed within 40 characters on both sides, as does a deletion or an insertion with no place found exactly; an accepted insertion, or a saved deletion turned back, also leaves it when the words right beside it on one side changed, so that less than 12 characters of whole words there read as before; and a saved change undone by hand becomes a suggestion again only where its words are plainly its own. Where a copy of a hunk's words stood right beside them and another editor took that copy out, the file reads exactly as with the hunk's own words taken out, so the change is taken as made: in the review's random edits, every side still changed was of this kind but 2 in about 28,000, where a word typed beside the hunk repeated the word on its other side. A dense run of decisions all applied in the file together can leave a deletion or an insertion among them without plain surroundings, so the round-trip properties with empty sides keep hunks 41 characters apart, and the properties over dense chapters check only that nothing is written or flipped by a guess. The anchor quality is unchanged: words that occur more than once are `ambiguous`, with the "?" after the ghost, even where their surroundings place them exactly.

Checked by unit and property tests, a mutation pass and `dev:clean` windows before and after the change; the figures are under `apply.23` in 11.2 (M6).

## Appendix D: release 1.0 answers

The author's answers to 11.6, dated, numbered as there. A question not listed here is still open.

- **1. Name** (2026-10-14): **Lapidary.** The author had become unsure about Igneous once it turned out to be in use elsewhere, and chose Lapidary from this research:
  - **Trademarks.** Rubrik's software mark IGNEOUS (US Reg. 5926931) was cancelled on 2026-06-19. Every live IGNEOUS mark in the US and Canada covers unrelated goods.
  - **Same niche.** Two free Markdown apps named Igneous, both aimed at Obsidian users, launched in the ten days before: a notes app for iPhone, iPad and Mac that opens Obsidian folders (getigneous.com, 2026-10-07), and a GNOME desktop editor described as an alternative frontend to Obsidian (github.com/H4RL33/igneous, 2026-10-05).
  - **Recommendation.** Rename for confusion and discoverability, not legal risk. **Lapidary** is first: the craft of cutting and polishing stone, obsidian included, and "lapidary" prose is polished and concise. It has no software trademark, no Obsidian plugin or theme, no notable app, and lapidary.app is taken. **Ventifact**, a stone polished by wind-blown sand over years, is the cleanest name but obscure. **Stet** looked clean on narrow checks only.
  - **Ruled out.** Whetstone, which is crowded with 2026 AI writing tools. Basalt, Pumice, Granite and Onyx, which are already Obsidian plugin or theme names.
- **2. Logo** (2026-10-14): concept A "Seam" is the direction, but with a more rock-like body, like obsidian, in place of the flat basalt fill. The squarish-round pebble shape stays. Three variants with the same rim and foreground are in `dist/logo-concepts/rock/`, with `compare.png` and each folder's `render.py`:
  - **conchoidal:** black glass with curved fracture ripples, ranked first as the most rock-like;
  - **polished-glass:** the cleanest at small sizes, but reads more like a black gem;
  - **golden-sheen:** reads as amber.

  At 16 to 32 px all three become a glossy black pebble with the ember rim; texture shows from 48 px. The author did not take these. He asked instead for a round rock divided into sections by fracture lines, after his own sketch, with Seam's foreground on top, in orange or perhaps a turquoise or cyan theme. If none of those suits, the original Seam stays. Prototypes go in `dist/logo-concepts/sections/`.
  - **Decided later on 2026-10-14: the original Seam, in turquoise** (rim #5ad3db to #178e9c, body #29393c to #121b1d, ghost #7d999b, line #f1efe6, dots #4fd8e0), drawn by `dist/logo-concepts/seam-colours/render.py` as palette 2. The ember original is the alternative he may return to. The sectioned-rock prototypes in `dist/logo-concepts/sections/` were not taken.
- **3. Repository** (2026-10-14): yes to a fresh public repository, `lapidary`, but later. `opensource.9` waits until the author asks for it.
- **8. Anthropic's Commercial Terms** (2026-10-14): **not accepted.** He will not make money from the app and will not bind himself legally for a free tool a handful of people may use. Each user installs Claude Code themselves and uses their own subscription or key; the app only runs the Claude Code the user installed. Until then the SDK had bundled `claude.exe` into the installer; the plan now ships no Claude Code binary (11.1, `setup.3`, `release.4`). Whether the SDK's JavaScript library also goes is question 21. The research behind the question is in Appendix A.
- **13. Setup** (2026-10-14): settled by question 8. Setup leads with installing Claude Code, then signing in.
- **Questions 4 to 7, 9 to 12 and 14 to 22** (2026-10-14): all as recommended.
  - **4. Identity.** Public commits and the package author use the GitHub noreply address; the author turns on "Keep my email address private" and "Block command line pushes that expose my email" when the public repo is made. Commits in the private repo keep his usual identity, because the public repo starts from a fresh root commit.
  - **5. Licence.** MIT, "Copyright (c) 2026 Michael Bradet-Legris", with his name as the installer's publisher.
  - **6. AGENTS.md.** Replaced by a pointer to CLAUDE.md (`opensource.7`).
  - **7. Commits.** The agent commits locally on a branch at the end of each milestone; the author reviews, merges and pushes. Agents never push. **Changed later on 2026-10-14:** the agent may commit and push. It works on a branch per milestone, pushes it, and fast-forwards `master` once the milestone verifies; it never force-pushes.
  - **9. Theme.** Yes to the darker light-theme tokens (#8a8378, #84681c), links in the app's blue, "Match system" as the default, and the warm basalt dark palette.
  - **10. Backups.** The newest 20 saves plus the first of each day for 30 days, in the app's data folder.
  - **11. Save.** Ctrl+S saves to the chapter, the button reads "Save N changes to chapter", leaving a chapter always asks, and there is no automatic save to chapter.
  - **12. Model.** The author's own setting moves from `opus` to Default; a new user on Pro gets Sonnet preselected, with the limits note.
  - **14. Updates.** Check at start-up and daily, with a toggle in About; download only on a click.
  - **15. Signing.** Decided only if Smart App Control actually blocks the friend; `release.14` stays conditional. No paid Apple program.
  - **16. OpenAI.** Ships labelled experimental; `hardening.11b` is cut.
  - **17. Diagnostics.** May include folder paths with the username replaced by `~`, chapter file names and the chapter count.
  - **18. Usage.** The implementer may spend a little of the author's Claude usage on verification: a few tiny reviews and connection tests.
  - **19. Sittings.** Yes to all four.
  - **20. Reports.** The friend messages the author on Discord or opens a GitHub issue; the issue form stays.
  - **21. SDK library.** Removed. The app runs the user's installed `claude` command directly (11.3 q, `cli.1`), so nothing of Anthropic's ships.
  - **22. Colour.** Turquoise for now, chosen with the logo (question 2); the ember palette may come back later.
- **Friend defaults** (2026-10-14, replacing the `files.17` questionnaire). The author would rather not question his friend, so the plan assumes:
  - **Platform.** Windows 11 x64. Smart App Control is handled only if he reports a block. macOS and Linux builds are cut (`release.16`, `release.17`, `brand.10`) unless he turns out to need one.
  - **Obsidian syntax** is shown as plain text, as now, with nothing rendered. The only special handling is that the reviewer is told to leave it alone, and a suggestion that would change the syntax itself, such as the target inside `[[...]]`, is kept out, so links and embeds keep working (`files.6` to `files.8`). Dataview and Templater get no special handling.
  - **Files.** One chapter per file, so `files.14` is cut. Save does not keep the file's creation date (`files.15` cut; the README says so). A chapter renamed while open is found again when reopened (`files.1`), so `files.18` is cut.
  - **Sync.** Any tool; the README's sync advice covers them.
  - **First use.** No sample chapter or screen share: the author sends the release link (`files.13`).
  - **No AI subscription.** He can use the OpenAI provider with a pay-as-you-go API key from platform.openai.com (a ChatGPT subscription cannot be used by other apps), or Claude Code with an Anthropic API key. At the 2026-09-11 prices this is roughly 5 US cents per chapter with gpt-5-mini and up to about a dollar with the largest models, depending on length and effort. The README and the Setup screen say so.

## Appendix A: billing and policy sources (checked 2026-09-08, re-checked 2026-10-16)

The list below is the 2026-09-08 check. Every page in it still loaded on 2026-10-16; what has changed since, and what the release depends on, is in the subsection after it.

- Help Center, "Use the Claude Agent SDK with your Claude plan": update dated June 15 says the planned change is paused; Agent SDK, `claude -p`, and third-party app usage still draw from the subscription's usage limits. https://support.claude.com/en/articles/15036540-use-the-claude-agent-sdk-with-your-claude-plan
- Claude Code docs, Legal and compliance: OAuth is for subscription holders' ordinary use of Claude Code and native apps; developers may not offer claude.ai login in their own products or intermediate credentials; an end user signing in to the unmodified Claude Code binary with their own subscription is allowed. https://code.claude.com/docs/en/legal-and-compliance
- Agent SDK overview and quickstart: bundled native binary, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` as the documented developer path. https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/overview and https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/quickstart
- Authentication: credential precedence, Windows storage location. https://code.claude.com/docs/en/authentication
- Structured outputs, streaming, cost tracking, TypeScript reference: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/structured-outputs , https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/streaming-output , https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/cost-tracking , https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/typescript

### Anthropic terms for a free open-source release (research dated 2026-10-14, re-read 2026-10-16; 11.6 question 8)

Quotes were checked by a second, independent pass on 2026-10-14, and again on 2026-10-16 against copies of the pages saved that day. This is information for the author, not legal advice.

- **Re-read on 2026-10-16.** Every page below loaded, and none has changed in a way that matters here since the 2026-10-14 research. Each was compared with its newest Wayback capture, dated 2026-10-04 to 2026-10-14; the Consumer Terms and Commercial Terms only by their unchanged effective dates. The legal page's only change since 2026-08-30 is its Healthcare compliance (BAA) section, rewritten between 2026-10-04 and 2026-10-08. The bullets below also record text that was already on these pages on 2026-10-14 but was left out then.
- **Legal and compliance page.** Running Claude Code "in your products or services" requires agreeing to the Commercial Terms "unless we've mutually agreed otherwise". The conditions are: the binary is unmodified, no built-in sign-in method is restricted, and no paying for, reselling or intermediating users' usage. The page's only example of such a product is hosted sandboxes or other agent infrastructure. The same page says Anthropic does not permit "third-party developers to offer Claude.ai login into their own applications", but does not prevent an end user "signing in to the unmodified Claude Code binary with their own Claude subscription". That section first appears in Wayback between 2026-08-16 and 2026-08-30. A 2026-02-18 capture instead said subscription OAuth tokens in any other tool, "including the Agent SDK", were not permitted; that sentence was gone by 2026-04-13. https://code.claude.com/docs/en/legal-and-compliance
  - **Using the Claude Code name and logo.** A product may say accurately, in plain text, that it has Claude Code preinstalled or that it "runs Claude Code". It may not use the Claude Code or Anthropic names or logos in its own product, feature or company name, in its logo, or in a way that suggests Anthropic built, endorses or partners with it. Any other use of Anthropic's names or logos falls under the Trademark Guidelines and needs written permission. The paragraph is in every capture from 2026-08-30 on. It sits in the section on products that run Claude Code under the Commercial Terms, and it is the only Anthropic text found on plain-text mentions; the plan follows it as guidance. Its rule is the plan's: no Claude or Anthropic marks in the product name or logo, and Claude Code named only in plain text. `DISCLAIMER` uses the page's own wording, that the app runs Claude Code.
  - **Also on the page since at least 2026-08-30, not recorded before.** Developers may not route requests through Free, Pro or Max plan credentials on behalf of their users. They may not collect, store or intermediate Claude.ai credentials or session tokens, and sign-in to a Claude account "must complete through Anthropic's own flow". Anthropic may enforce these restrictions "without prior notice". The advertised Pro and Max limits "assume ordinary, individual usage" of Claude Code and the Agent SDK. Under License, Free, Pro and Max users are under the Consumer Terms, and Team, Enterprise and API users under the Commercial Terms.
- **Anthropic Trademark Guidelines** (effective 2024-08-01; identical to the 2026-10-04 capture). They cover Anthropic's names, logos, brand elements and anything it uses to identify its products. Anthropic's marks may be used only as it specifically permits, in materials it approves beforehand, and never in a way that implies sponsorship, endorsement, or a relationship or affiliation with Anthropic. Marks may not be altered or carry a trademark symbol. The guidelines say nothing about plain-text mentions of a product; the legal page's paragraph above is the only text found on that case. https://www.anthropic.com/legal/trademark-guidelines
- **Consumer Terms** (effective 2025-10-08), which govern Free, Pro and Max users of Claude Code. Section 3 bars accessing the Services "through automated or non-human means", except with an Anthropic API key or "where we otherwise explicitly permit it". The app runs `claude -p` as a child process, which is scripted access. The texts treated as that explicit permission are the legal page's clause that an end user may sign in to the unmodified binary with their own subscription, and the Help Center's 2026-10-07 statement that `claude -p` and third-party apps can still use subscription limits. Section 12, "Use of our brand", bars using Anthropic's name, logos or trademarks in connection with other products, or in a way that implies affiliation, endorsement or sponsorship, without prior written permission. The legal page's name-and-logo paragraph is the only Anthropic text that addresses a plain-text statement that a product runs Claude Code; whether its section applies to this app is the open question in section 9 risk 8, bullet 4. https://www.anthropic.com/legal/consumer-terms
- **Agent SDK overview and quickstart** (actively maintained; reworded between April and August 2026 and kept after the August rewrite of the legal page; on 2026-10-16 the overview matched its 2026-10-10 capture, and the quickstart differed from its 2026-10-04 capture by one word). Unless previously approved, Anthropic does not allow third-party developers "to offer claude.ai login or rate limits for their products", agents built on the SDK included, and points them to API keys instead. The SDK "is governed by Anthropic's Commercial Terms of Service", including in products offered to others. https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/overview
  - **Branding guidelines** on the overview page, written for partners integrating the Agent SDK. Claude branding is optional. Allowed: "Claude Agent", "Claude" inside a menu already labelled "Agents", and "{YourAgentName} Powered by Claude". Not permitted: "Claude Code" or "Claude Code Agent", or visuals that mimic Claude Code. The product keeps its own branding and must not appear to be Claude Code or any Anthropic product. These rules address SDK integrations, which the app no longer is (C.42, Appendix D 21). It meets the last one anyway: its name, logo and window are its own, and its provider label is the plain "Claude". A GitHub issue asking for the same guidance for projects that run the Claude Code CLI, anthropics/claude-code#24600, was closed as not planned on 2026-03-11 with no answer from Anthropic.
- **Commercial Terms** (effective 2025-06-17; no newer version posted on 2026-10-16). Nothing in them depends on charging money, and no page exempts free or open-source projects. The author would be the Customer and the people he gives the app to his Users. Acceptance is free: signing in to the Console says "By continuing, you agree". D.3: the Customer must notify Users that factual assertions in outputs should be checked before they are relied on; the README's `AI_OUTPUT_NOTICE` says so anyway, as plain good practice. K.2: the Customer defends Anthropic against third-party claims about the Customer's or Users' inputs, or about use that breaches the Usage Policy, and pays resulting judgments or approved settlements. This duty is uncapped (L.3 excludes it), while other liability is capped both ways at fees paid. Section F grants no rights by implication; the legal page's clause is the only text that permits shipping `claude.exe`. https://www.anthropic.com/legal/commercial-terms
- **Usage Policy** effective 2026-11-12, published on anthropic.com on 2026-10-08 and so not yet in force on 2026-10-16. The version it replaces, effective 2025-09-15, has no such clause. Under "Do Not Abuse Our Platform" it bars reselling, proxying or otherwise providing access to Claude through unauthorised means, including services that "route requests through consumer subscriptions or misrepresent the product or client being used". The app runs the real `claude` binary and never sets or overrides its client or entrypoint identity (CLAUDE.md rule 6). Anthropic's announcement of the new version, "2026 Usage Policy update" (2026-10-08), lists its changes without mentioning this clause. The same version names Claude Code users and the end users of products that integrate Claude among the people it binds, and its bypass bullet now includes circumventing "the account sign-up or log-in process"; the app never starts or handles a sign-in. https://www.anthropic.com/legal/aup and https://www.anthropic.com/news/2026-usage-policy-update
- **Help Center, Agent SDK with your plan.** Update of 2026-10-07: Max and Team plans now include monthly API credits that cover the Agent SDK, `claude -p`, the Claude API and Claude Managed Agents, and the SDK, `claude -p` and third-party apps can still be used "with your subscription limits". The June 15 notice that the planned change is paused is still there. The page's earlier text, including the promise of notice before changes, is gone. https://support.claude.com/en/articles/15036540-use-the-claude-agent-sdk-with-your-claude-plan
- **Help Center, Monthly API credits for Max and Team plans** (new on 2026-10-07). The credits cover `claude -p` and the Agent SDK when they run with an API key from the linked Console organisation. Signed in with a Claude plan instead, `claude -p` and Agent SDK usage "still draw from your plan's usage limits". That is how the app's reviews run: a review counts against the user's plan limits, or is billed to their key when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is set. https://support.claude.com/en/articles/17154008-monthly-api-credits-for-max-and-team-plans
- **API credits for subscribers** (platform docs, first archived 2026-10-07). Max 5x $100 and Max 20x $200 a month; Team $20 per Standard seat and $100 per Premium seat, pooled and capped at $500; Free, Pro and Enterprise are not eligible. Its coverage table lists the Claude API, Claude Managed Agents, the Agent SDK and the Playground as covered, and Claude Code, extra usage in the Claude apps and other platforms as not covered. This page does not mention `claude -p`, while the Help Center says the credits cover it when run with a key from the linked organisation; that was not tested. The README and Setup must not say the credits pay for the app's reviews. https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/about-claude/api-credits-for-subscribers
- **Enforcement in 2026.** In January, Anthropic said third-party harnesses using subscriptions are prohibited (https://venturebeat.com/technology/anthropic-cracks-down-on-unauthorized-claude-usage-by-third-party-harnesses). In March, OpenCode, a free open-source tool, removed its Pro/Max login after "anthropic legal requests", along with an Anthropic prompt file and other Anthropic references (https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode/pull/18186). In April, third-party tools such as OpenClaw were moved off plan limits onto separately billed extra usage (https://techcrunch.com/2026/04/04/anthropic-says-claude-code-subscribers-will-need-to-pay-extra-for-openclaw-support/). In May and June, a separate billing plan for Agent SDK usage was announced and then paused. Searched through 2026-10-16: no report of action against an app that runs the unmodified binary with each user's own sign-in.
- **Asking Anthropic.** "Contact sales" leads to an Enterprise checkout with no form for individuals. A support request is a courtesy; silence is not approval.

### OpenAI (research dated 2026-09-11, Phase 5c)

- OpenAI's documented route for a third-party app is an API key from a Platform account, billed per token to that account. That is what the OpenAI provider uses. The SDK reads `OPENAI_API_KEY` by default and the app passes a stored key explicitly. https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/structured-outputs and https://platform.openai.com/docs/api-reference/responses
- Signing in with a ChatGPT subscription (Plus, Pro, Team) inside a third-party app has no stated permission. The only subscription sign-in OpenAI documents is for its own Codex tools; public requests for a "Sign in with ChatGPT" for other apps are still open feature requests, and routing a Codex login through another app is described in the community as unsupported. So the app offers no ChatGPT sign-in and says in the dialog that OpenAI reviews are billed per token, not to a ChatGPT subscription. Sources seen: https://github.com/openai/codex/issues/10974 (404 when re-checked on 2026-10-16) , https://community.openai.com/t/login-with-chatgpt-allow-users-to-use-their-own-plus-subscription-in-3rd-party-apps/1378506 , https://community.openai.com/t/api-chatgpt-subscription-cannot-use-api-with-chatgpt-subscription/875542
- Prices for the cost estimate, standard tier: https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/pricing (copied into `src/shared/ai/pricing.ts` on 2026-09-11).
